**Built in step 26d: resume** (`packages/data/src/profile.ts`: version 5, `WatchProgress`, `MAX_PROGRESS`, `isWatchProgress`, the `progress` edit; `apps/tv/src/movies`: `movie-progress.ts`, the details' `point`, `actions` and `refresh`, `Movies.progress`; `player-state.ts`: `MoviePlay.fromMs`, `PlayingMovie.fromMs`, the `progressed` and `lifecycle` options; `main.tsx`'s wiring):
- **Where a movie was left** is the profile's `progress` (§1.3; version 5, migration 4 → 5 adds it empty): by ContentKey, the media time, the length when known (the engine's once it has a picture, else the panel's) and when it was saved. The latest 500 are kept: a new one past the cap drops the one saved longest ago, never itself. The app reads them once at the start (`createMovieProgress().load`) and writes one movie at a time through the query worker (`editProfile` `{ kind: 'progress' }`, a read-modify-write each); a save equal to what is kept writes nothing.
- **When the player saves** (`PlayerOptions.progressed`): every 10 s while the movie plays (`SAVE_EVERY_MS`, counted from its play or the last save; never while it opens, buffers or is paused), and when it fails, ends, is left (Back, STOP, the end card's "Back", a dispose) or the app hides (`lifecycle` `hidden`). The position is the stream's while it shows a picture, else where it was last seen or asked to start (a "Resume" left before its picture keeps its place); a seek still waiting for the keys to rest doesn't count.
- **The app hiding:** the player saves first, then reads no position until the app shows again. webOS and the desktop let the stream go on hide (`Html5Engine.suspend` empties the `<video>`, so its clock says 0) while the controller still says "playing": without this a hidden app's next save (or a catch-up's seek bar) would read 0. The player subscribes to the lifecycle when it is made, before the controller (made on the first play), so its save runs before the engine lets go.
- **Watched** (`isWatched`, `keptPoint`): a movie that ended, or was left within its last 2 minutes or past 95 % of its length (whichever comes first: 95 % for films over 40 minutes), keeps no place; nor one left before 10 s (`MIN_RESUME_MS`: it starts over, no "Resume from 0:04"). Without a length only the end and the 10 s apply.
- **A play that doesn't know the length** ("Resume" pressed before the details' read came back) keeps the length already kept with the place: it plays with it (the seek bar shows it) and saves it, so the tile keeps its bar. The e2e found this: the place was saved with no length and the tile's bar went.
- **The details:** a movie left part way offers "Resume from 12:34" first (focused; the time is one argument, `i18n.duration`), then "Play from the start" and "Back"; any other offers "Play" and "Back". "Resume" plays from the place (`MoviePlay.fromMs`, the engine's `startMs`). Back from the player reads the place again (`MovieDetails.refresh`): the buttons follow and the first is focused ("Resume" from the new place, or "Play" once it was watched).
- **The grid:** a tile draws its bar (`Tile.progress`) from the share watched when the length is known (`Movies.progress`, which reads the places' version so the tiles draw again when one changes). The bar fills from the inline start: from the right in Arabic, as the UI kit's tile bar always has (only the player's seek bar is a left-to-right media timeline).
- **Decided (not gates):** the 10 s floor; a place kept per movie key (a provider's renumbered movie loses its place, as favourites would without their re-match; not worth one for resume); a profile write every 10 s of playing (the query worker's IndexedDB, off the UI thread; the profile is well under 300 KB).
- **Tests:** 4,359 unit tests in the full local run (+27). `profile.test.ts` (+3: version 5 and its migration; a stored version 4 document upgraded on load with its snapshot, then a place saved into it; the edit: kept, moved, the length learnt, taken away, the same place, only a place's fields, 13 kinds of no place; the cap of 500, never the one being saved, a document over the cap); `query-service.test.ts` (the edit over RPC, version 5 after the upgrades); `movie-progress.test.ts` (6: the watched rule at its edges, the kept point, the start's profile read, the saves with the known length, the version, a failed write); `player-state.test.ts` (+7: from `fromMs`; every 10 s only while playing; Back, a failure, STOP, a waiting seek; the end; the app hiding and showing; channels and catch-up never, and the lifecycle let go on dispose); `movie-details.test.ts` (+2: the three buttons and what each plays; `refresh`); `movies-state.test.ts` (+1: the bars and their redraw); `catalogs.test.ts` (+8: the two new strings). e2e `movie-resume.spec.ts` (en-US, ar-SA: leave at 5:30 by keys, "Resume from 5:30" focused with "Play from the start" and "Back" on one row beside the poster, the profile's place, the tile's bar at 55.4 % from the inline start, "Resume" asks from 5:30, a relaunch keeps it all, "Resume" with the panel's read held plays with the kept length, the app hiding keeps 5:40 while the player is open, "Play from the start" from 0:00 and left at once takes the place away); `player-media.spec.ts` +1 (`e2e:media`: Big Buck Bunny left five minutes in resumes inside its MP4 within 5 s, the frames moving). `guides.spec.ts` and `onboarding.spec.ts` now check version 4 or later. Screenshots `movie-resume-{en-US,ar-SA}`, `movie-resume-grid-{en-US,ar-SA}`, `player-movie-resumed-en` looked at.
- **Mutation:** `artifacts/mutate26d.py`, 81 mutants written before the run (the profile's version 5, its migration and cap, `isWatchProgress` and the edit; the watched rule and the kept point; the start's read, the share and the saves; the player's saves, its hidden flag and the lifecycle; the details' buttons and `refresh`; the grid's bars): 73 caught (`mutate26d.log`). The eight that lived were test gaps, now closed: a value that isn't an object, an endless position with no length, a stored `undefined`, a stored start, a place past its length, the same place with a new length, a new play long after a save, and a relaunch while hidden. Re-run 8/8 (`mutate26d-b.log`). `artifacts/mutate26d-e2e.py` 11/11 (the details' words, the tile's bar, and each piece of `main.tsx`'s wiring).
- **Size:** main's own code 374.4 → 377.5 KB (Tizen) and 361.7 → 364.8 KB (webOS); the query worker 110.0 KB; app.css 82.2 KB; 219 core-js modules on both.

**Built in step 26e: "Recently added"** (`packages/data/src`: `recent.ts` (`RecentList`, `mergeRecent`, `RECENT_MAX`, `RECENT_KINDS`), `catalog-store.ts` (`putCatalogKind`'s `recent`, `readRecent`), `ingest-version.ts` (`INGEST_VERSION` 2), `QueryService.recent`; `DataEngine.recent`; `refresh-scheduler.ts`'s `catalogRefreshDue`; `apps/tv/src/movies`: `movies-state.ts`'s rail entry (`RecentEntry`, `MovieEntry`) and `MoviesScreen.tsx`'s label; the mock's films' `added_days_ago`):
- **The data:** the ingest keeps each source's 100 newest movies by their panel's date (Xtream `added`, epoch seconds), newest first, in a `RecentList`: a sorted array that a full list lets only a strictly newer movie into, so equal dates (a panel's bulk import) keep the panel's order. Each kept item is a copy whose strings own their text, so no decoded chunk stays pinned for the whole ingest. The list is stored in the movie kind's categories record (`catalog_cats` `[src, gen, 'movie']` holds `{ cats, recent }`), so it is written, swapped and deleted with its generation. No item gains a field: the chunks are as they were.
- **The query:** `recent({ sources, kind, limit })` reads each source's list (one record each, in one transaction) and merges them: newest first, at most 100 (`RECENT_MAX`); on an equal date the source listed first, then the panel's order (no reliance on `Array.prototype.sort` being stable, which it isn't on Chromium 53-68). No chunk is read and nothing scans a catalog. A larger or broken `limit` is refused, not cut.
- **The cached catalogs are read again:** `INGEST_VERSION` is 2, in a module of its own (`ingest-version.ts`, exported through `@lablo/data/client`). A refresh rebuilds a catalog that version 1 built even from unchanged bytes, and the refresh schedule makes such a catalog due at its first check (`catalogRefreshDue`: 15 s after the start, not up to 12 h later). Until it has run, "Recently added" isn't shown (the record has no list).
- **The screen:** "Recently added" is the rail's first entry, before "All movies", when the sources have a dated movie, and the screen opens on it. Its movies (at most 100) are read with the categories on each open and held while the screen is open: it has no chunks, so its posters are asked at the rest after the open with no key pressed. A catalog swap keeps "Recently added", "All movies" or a category (by its source and name) on show as the entries move; "Recently added" gone, the first entry from its first movie. The label is ours (`movies.recent`: "Recently added", «أضيف حديثًا»).
- **Decided (not gates):**
  - **A playlist's movies are left out:** an M3U line has no date. A "first refresh that saw it" date would give every movie of a source's first refresh the same date, so the list would be the playlist's own order, not the newest, and it would need every movie's first sighting kept across refreshes (up to 120,000 keys a source). A screen of playlists alone has no "Recently added".
  - **The screen opens on "Recently added",** not on "All movies" below it: the newest movies are what a returning viewer looks for, and "All movies" (the categories in turn) is two keys away.
  - **100 movies,** per source and in all (17 rows of six).
  - **Parental control (step 38) must filter this list too:** it holds movies of every category, adult ones included (each item's `group` names its category).
- **Tests:** 4,391 unit tests (+32). `recent.test.ts` (8: the newest 100 of 250 out of order, equal dates in the order they came and a full list taking only a newer one, no date or none after 1970, the kept copies; the merge across lists, equal dates by the list given first, any date, a missing list); `catalog-store.test.ts` (+1: the record with and without its list, `readRecent`, dropped with its generation); `query-service.test.ts` (+5: the merge in source order, the sources asked once and their loaded generations, a catalog an older version built or another kind, the cap and a broken limit, over RPC); `ingest.test.ts` (+2: version 2; a playlist keeps an empty list); `test/ingest-mock.test.ts` (+1: the mock's six films newest first with their panel dates and stored items, a playlist adds none, and a catalog stamped version 1 without its list rebuilt from the same bytes); `refresh-scheduler.test.ts` (+1: due at once for an older version, not for a newer one, a record without a stamp or one whose refresh failed); `movies-state.test.ts` (+10: the rail and the first focus, none without dates, the posters at the rest with no key, to "All movies" and back, OK and the bars, the read held and failed, a swap's new films' posters with the focus unmoved, nothing held when a swap leaves no movies, a category by source and name, the entries moving through swaps). e2e `movies.spec.ts` (en-US, ar-SA) opens on "Recently added": the mock's films by their `added` (Big Buck Bunny, Sintel, the multi-audio film, Elephants Dream, Tears of Steel, the 4K pattern; the mock's fixture now dates them 1 to 13 days back, Big Buck Bunny and Sintel still the first two tiles), then our 40, the posters as before, then "All movies" in the panel's order and "Test Patterns". `movies-throttled.spec.ts` opens on "Recently added" (the 100 newest of 20,000, every 30th movie) and goes to "All movies" for its walk: opening 64 ms full / 42 ms low tier at 4x, "OK on "All movies"" 32 / 26 ms, every press within 150 ms. Screenshots `movies-{en-US,ar-SA}` and `movies-all-en-US` looked at.
- **Mutation:** `artifacts/mutate26e.py`, 57 mutants written before the run (the cap and kinds, `RecentList.push` and its copies, `mergeRecent`; the record and its read; the ingest's kinds, push, write and dates; the version; the query's limit, sources, merge, load and handler; the schedule's version check; the screen's entry, held movies, rest, clean-ups and swap refs): 53 caught (`mutate26e.log`). Of the four that lived, one was equivalent (a full list's `<=` written as `<`: an equal date goes last and is dropped again) and three were test gaps, now closed (a merge over dates of 0 and below; a swap that keeps "Recently added" at the same place, where only the load's own rest asks the new films' posters, since the navigator tells nobody of a focus that didn't move; nothing held once a swap leaves no movies): re-run 3/3 (`mutate26e-b.log`). `artifacts/mutate26e-e2e.py` 7/7 (the label, the engine's call, both catalogs' words, the mock's dates).
- **Size:** main's own code 377.5 → 378.4 KB (Tizen) and 364.8 → 365.7 KB (webOS); the ingest worker 127.9 → 129.0 KB, the query worker 110.0 → 110.9 KB; app.css 82.2 KB; 219 core-js modules on both.

**Built in step 27a: the series screen** (step 27 was split in leg 71 into 27a, this; 27b, a show's details with its seasons and episodes; 27c, playing an episode, its progress and auto-next; 27d, "Continue watching". `apps/tv/src/movies`: `movies-state.ts`'s `kind`, `SERIES_SCREEN`, `POSTER_SCREENS`, `Movies.episodes`, `selectSeries`, `episodeLabel`; `MoviesScreen.tsx`'s `POSTER_WORDS` and `POSTER_TEST_IDS`; the live header's "Series"; `packages/data/src`: `RECENT_KINDS`, `INGEST_VERSION` 3; the mock's shows' `added_days_ago`):
- **One screen for two kinds:** `createMovies({ kind: 'series' })` is the movies screen for the sources' series: its own screen id (`series`), its reads by kind (`categories`, `chunk`, `recent`), its own words ("Series", "All series", "Recently updated", "{n} series", «المسلسلات», «كل المسلسلات», «حُدّثت مؤخرًا») and test ids (`series-screen`, `series-rail`, `series-grid`, tiles `show-<i>`), and `data-kind` on the screen. Everything else is the movies screen's: the rail and the 2:3 grid, the poster caps by tier, the keys and Back, a catalog swap.
- **The header:** "Guide" 0, "Movies" 1, "Series" 2, "Settings" 3. "Series" opens in every state of the live list, as "Movies" does. The specs that reach "Settings" press once more (13 places in 5 specs); the old-engine walk reaches only "Guide" and "Movies".
- **"Recently updated":** a panel's show is dated by its `last_modified` (else `added`), which moves when the show gains an episode, so the series rail's first entry ("Recently updated", when a show has a date) lists the shows with new episodes first. The ingest keeps each source's 100 newest shows as it does movies (`RECENT_KINDS` `['movie', 'series']`), and `INGEST_VERSION` 3 rebuilds the catalogs version 2 built at their first check.
- **A playlist's series:** each `/series/` line is an episode with its own URL, one tile each, in the playlist's order. An entry whose every part is a playlist's (`Movies.episodes`) counts episodes ("7 episodes", «7 حلقات»), not shows. OK on one opens the movie details (the line's facts) and plays it as a movie (`selectSeries`: an item with a URL is an episode; an Xtream show has none and opens its own details from step 27b; until then OK on a show does nothing). Its place, "Resume from" and the tile's bar are a movie's (by the line's key).
- **The tile's season and episode mark** (`episodeLabel`): a name that ends in a mark ("Show S01 E01", "S1E2", "s01.e01" after a space or a separator) shows the name over the mark, in the subtitle, so the poster tile's two lines never cut the one part that tells a playlist's episodes apart. The first screenshot showed five tiles all reading "Lablo Test Series S01" or "S02". A name with no mark, or nothing before it, stays whole. Display only: nothing groups the episodes.
- **Decided in leg 71 (not gates):**
  - **A playlist's episodes are listed flat, not grouped into shows by their names:** grouping would need every line of a category before its first tile shows (a panel lists 60k episodes), a new stored structure, and a parser for many naming styles. Most playlists with series come from Xtream panels, and step 37's M3U→Xtream upgrade turns those into real shows. Auto-next is for Xtream shows only. Revisit if users ask.
  - **Series join "Recently added" as "Recently updated"** (a version bump, one more record per source).
  - **One screen for both kinds**, not a copy: the series screen gains every fix the movies screen gets.
- **Tests:** 4,440 unit tests in the full local run (+49). `movies-state.test.ts` (+7: the screen's id and its reads by kind, a movies screen's unchanged; the series screen following its own focus moves; the episodes count for a playlist's entries, "All series" of playlists alone, a panel's entries and "Recently updated", a movies screen never; the playlists read again on a catalog swap; OK to `select`; `selectSeries` for an episode and a show; `episodeLabel`'s spellings and the names it keeps whole); `live-state.test.ts` (+1, one changed: the four buttons in order, "Series" opening in every state of the list, "Settings" at 3); `recent.test.ts`, `ingest.test.ts` (version 3; a playlist keeps an empty series list); `test/ingest-mock.test.ts` (+1: the mock's two shows newest first by their `last_modified`, the stored items, no URL, a playlist none); `refresh-scheduler.test.ts` (version 3; versions 1 and 2 due at once); `catalogs.test.ts` (the new strings); `test/engines-onboarding.test.ts` (the old-engine harness's marks, now by `POSTER_TEST_IDS`). e2e `series.spec.ts` (en-US, ar-SA): a panel's shows ("Series" in the header with all four buttons on one line in the safe area, the screen's title, test ids and kind, the rail "Recently updated", "All series" and the category, "Recently updated · 2 series", the Arabic show first and each cover asked once, the grid from the inline start, OK on a show doing nothing yet, "All series" in the panel's order, Back to the rail and to the header) and a playlist's episodes (no "Recently updated", "All series · 7 episodes", seven tiles each with its "S01 E01" uncut, OK on the first to its details and "Play" asking `/series/…/3001.mkv`, Back to the grid, the rail and the header). Screenshots `series-header-{en-US,ar-SA}`, `series-{en-US,ar-SA}`, `series-playlist-{en-US,ar-SA}` looked at.
- **Mutation:** `artifacts/mutate27a.py`, 40 mutants written before the run (the kind, the screen id and the reads by kind; the episodes count and its playlists; `selectSeries`; `episodeLabel`'s pattern and mark; the header's count, order and "Series"; `RECENT_KINDS`, `INGEST_VERSION`): 40 caught (`mutate27a.log`). `artifacts/mutate27a-e2e.py` 24/24 (the words, test ids and kind, the tile's mark, the header's button, the paint, `main.tsx`'s kind, `select` and `openSeries`, both catalogs, the mock's dates); writing its list added the spec's check of the screen's title.

**Built in step 27b: a show's details, its seasons and episodes** (`packages/data/src`: `panel-answers.ts` (`PanelAnswers`, now under `VodInfoCache` too), `series-info.ts` (`SeriesInfoCache`, `seriesInfoTarget`, `SERIES_INFO_TTL_MS`), `QueryService.seriesInfo`; `DataEngine.seriesInfo`; core's `XtreamSeriesInfo.releaseDate`; `apps/tv/src/shows`: `show-details.ts`, `ShowDetailsScreen.tsx`, `show.css`; `selectSeries`'s `show`; the catalogs' `show.*`):
- **The read:** `get_series_info` in the query worker when a show's details open, kept 24 h per show (`PanelAnswers`, the movies' rules: a failure isn't kept, a panel's "not found" is, two asks share one request). It keeps 20 shows, not 100: a show's answer holds all of its episodes. The panel is the show's source's (`sourceAccount`); the panel's id comes from the show's key (`x:<src>:series:<id>`, `seriesInfoTarget`), since a show's catalog item has no URL. A key of another source, a movie's or an episode's, an M3U key or a plain playlist get `null` with no request. The episodes' keys are of the show's source and their URLs of its account.
- **The screen** (pushed on the live navigator over the series screen by OK on a show's cover, painted instead of it): the series screen's header ("Series" and the clock), then a 300 px top band: the cover (200 × 300 px at the inline start; the title on its background when there is none or it fails) and beside it the title, the category, a line of the year and the rating ("2026 · Rated 8/10"), the genre and the plot (two lines). Under it, 24 px apart, a row of season chips (264 px, 16 px apart, six in view) and the season's episodes (88 px rows, 8 px apart, five in view): the number, the title over the plot (the provider's text, each in its own direction), and the length at the inline end ("5 min", «5 د»). Postcss-rtlcss mirrors it: the cover and the first season on the right in Arabic.
- **Seasons:** the panel's seasons that have episodes, in order. A chip says "Season 2" in the UI's language («الموسم 2») when the panel's name is empty or says only that ("Season 2", "season 02", "S2"), else the panel's own («الموسم ١», "Specials"). No episode count on a chip: the first screenshot read "Season 2  2" as one number.
- **Keys:** the screen opens on the first season with the focus on its first episode. UP from the first episode goes to the seasons; the season on show follows the focus along the row (every episode came with the show: no read) and its episodes start from the top. DOWN or OK on a season goes to its episodes, where the focus last was. OK on an episode does nothing yet (step 27c plays it). Back goes from the episodes to the season on show, and from the seasons closes the details (the series screen's focus is where it was). While the read is out, after a failure, or with no episodes there is nothing to focus, and Back closes.
- **States:** a spinner while the read is out (`data-pending` 1); a failed read keeps the catalog's facts with a note ("Couldn't read this show's episodes."), and opening the show again reads again; a panel that lists no episodes (or doesn't know the show) says "This show has no episodes yet." in the seasons' place. An answer for details that closed, or opened on another show since, is dropped.
- **Decided in leg 72 (not gates):**
  - **The layout:** a row of season chips over a full-width list of episodes, not a rail of seasons beside the list: the episodes keep the width for their titles and plots. Flexbox and margins only.
  - **No "Try again" on a failed read:** Back and OK on the show read again (failures aren't kept), the same two keys.
  - **OK on an episode does nothing until step 27c**, as OK on a show did before this step.
  - **The app's folder is `apps/tv/src/shows/`, not `series/`:** a spec's `page.route('**/series/**')` (the episodes' streams) would also hold the dev server's modules from a `/src/series/` folder. The playlist spec hung on its `page.goto` that way, and its pattern is now the panel's stream path (`/series/<user>/<pass>/`).
- **Tests:** 4,481 unit tests in the full local run (+41). `series-info.test.ts` (8: the target from the key and the source's account, and the keys that get none; the 24 h cache, another show's own answer, a shared request, no failure kept and the panel's "not found" kept, the 20 newest kept; `QueryService.seriesInfo` over RPC: an Xtream source, a `get.php` link's panel and keys, a plain playlist, a source not stored, another source's key, a movie's, a day later, no network; the panel's error without the URL); `vod-info.test.ts` unchanged on `PanelAnswers`; `xtream.test.ts` and `test/core-mock.test.ts` (the release date and its spellings); `show-details.test.ts` (13: the open, the read and the first season's episodes focused; the season following the focus and its episodes from the top; OK on a season; Back to the season on show, DOWN to the episode left, Back to the grid; a failed read and Back while reading; no episodes, no panel, not found; a stale answer; the cover and dispose; season names, episode lines, the seasons listed, the facts, the layout's numbers); `movies-state.test.ts` (`selectSeries`'s `show` now required). e2e `show-details.spec.ts` (en-US, ar-SA: the held read, the facts, the seasons and episodes, the geometry, season 2 by keys, OK on an episode, Back, the cache, a failed read then a read, the panel's Arabic season name; a show with no episodes); `series.spec.ts`'s OK on a show opens its details. Screenshots `show-details-*` looked at: the first ones showed the chips' counts read as one number with the season's, and the "no episodes" note floating under an empty row.
- **Mutation:** `artifacts/mutate27b.py`, 80 mutants written before the run (`PanelAnswers`, the target, the cache's size and day, the RPC handler, the release date, the facts, season names, episode lines, the seasons, the zones, Back, OK, the follower, stale answers, dispose): 76 caught at first (`mutate27b.log`). Two were test gaps, closed (the season pattern's `^`, which "Bonus 2" shows, and the screen id `show`; `mutate27b-b.log`), two equivalent (`fits` without the gap gives the same 6 and 5). `artifacts/mutate27b-e2e.py` 31/32, then 32/32 once the spec pinned the "no episodes" note's box (`mutate27b-e2e-b.log`); writing its list added the spec's exact geometry and the note's absence while reading and after a failure.

PR #78 merged as `9a6b9ee` on green CI (verify 8 m 9 s, engines 18 m 50 s, media 10 m 14 s on a rerun: the first tripped the 300 MB guide's declared-channels budget, 75 ms of 60, a timing flake this step doesn't touch).

**Built in step 27c1: playing an episode, its place, and the show's last episode** (step 27c was split in leg 73 into 27c1, this, and 27c2, auto-next. `packages/data/src/profile.ts`: version 6, `ShowProgress`, `MAX_SHOWS`, `isShowProgress`, the `episode` edit, `dropOldest`; `apps/tv/src/movies/movie-progress.ts`: `LastEpisode`, `lastEpisode`, `save`'s `show`; `apps/tv/src/shows`: `episode-play.ts` (`episodeItem`, `episodeRequest`, `episodeMark`, `episodePlay`, `episodePlace`, `nextPlace`, `resumePlace`), `show-details.ts`'s `play`, `progress`, `point`, `share` and `refresh`, `ShowDetailsScreen.tsx`'s rows; `player-state.ts`: `EpisodeMark`, `MoviePlay.episode`, `PlayingMovie.episode`, `progressed`'s show; `PlayerScreen.tsx`: the episode's line and end card; the catalogs' `show.mark`, `player.episodeEnded`):
- **Playing:** OK on an episode plays it in the movie player (`playMovie`): a `mode: 'vod'` request for its `/series/<user>/<pass>/<id>.<ext>` URL, keyed by the episode (`x:<src>:episode:<id>`), with the panel's length (else the one kept with its place). The seek bar, pause, the 10 s steps, FF/RW and the dead channel keys are a movie's. The info bar names the show, then "S1 E2 · title" (`show.mark`: "S{season} E{episode}", «م{season} ح{episode}»; the title in its own direction). Its end says "The episode has ended", the show and the episode, with "Back" (step 27c2 puts "Next" there).
- **Its place:** by the episode's key under the movies' rules (`progress`, every 10 s while it plays, on leave, stop, failure and hide; none before 10 s; watched within its last 2 minutes, past 95 % or at its end). Its row says "Resume from 2:34" in its plot's place and draws its bar along its bottom (`ProgressBar`, from the inline start, as a tile's); OK on it resumes from there (no "Play from the start" choice: LEFT and RW go back).
- **The show's last episode** is the profile's `shows` (version 6, migration 5 → 6 adds it empty): by the show's ContentKey, the episode played last, whether it was watched, and when it was saved (the latest 200 shows; a new one past the cap drops the one saved longest ago, never itself). One save of an episode is one write (`editProfile` `{ kind: 'episode', show, last, progress }`): its place and its show's entry together. The entry is written when the episode played changes or becomes watched, and its time again whenever the episode's place is written; the same entry and place write nothing.
- **Where the details land:** they open on the show's last episode, its season on show and the seasons' row on it (UP and Back go there); once it was watched, on the next one in the panel's order across seasons (`nextPlace`: the next in its season, else the next season's first); after the show's last episode, on it. A show never played, or whose last episode the panel no longer lists, opens on the first episode. Back from the player lands the same way (`ShowDetails.refresh`): on the episode left part way, or on the next once it ended.
- **Decided in leg 73 (not gates):**
  - **A `shows` map, not the show's key in each `progress` entry:** a watched episode keeps no place, so the place can't say which episode a show reached; the map keeps it (and "watched") for step 27d's "Continue watching" (a show by its last episode: resume it, or its next one).
  - **The entry's time follows its episode's place:** 27d orders shows by it.
  - **OK on a part-watched episode resumes it,** as a series player usually does; the row says so. A movie's details offer both because they have the room and a film is rarely restarted by seeking.
  - **The details land on the next episode after a watched one,** where OK plays what comes next.
  - **"The episode has ended" for now:** 27c2's card replaces it when a next episode exists.
- **Tests:** 4,508 unit tests in the full local run (+27). `profile.test.ts` (version 6 pinned; the 5 → 6 migration; a stored version 5 document upgraded on load with its snapshot, then an episode saved into it; the edit: the show's entry alone, with a place, watched, a place taken away, the entry alone changing, another show's, only the fields kept; nine kinds of no entry; the cap of 200; the future-version pins now 7); `query-service.test.ts` (the edit over RPC, version 6 after the upgrades); `movie-progress.test.ts` (+2: the start's `shows`, eight kinds of no entry; an episode's saves: left at once, part way, further on, watched by the kept length, ended, the next one, ended with no length, a position that isn't one, a movie); `episode-play.test.ts` (7: the item, the request, the mark, the play's length and start; the places, the next across seasons, where a show opens); `show-details.test.ts` (+4: OK plays with its place and season; the rows' places and their redraw; the open on the last episode, the next once watched across seasons, the last one staying, another show's, one no longer listed; `refresh`); `player-state.test.ts` (+2: the episode carried through a seek's new play and the engine's length; the show's key in every save). e2e `episode-resume.spec.ts` (en-US, ar-SA: OK on S1 E2 asks `/series/…/3002.mkv`, the info bar's show and "S1 E2 · Tone and Clock" («م1 ح2 · …»), four RIGHTs to 0:40, Back onto it with "Resume from 0:40" and its bar at 13.3 % from the inline start along the row's bottom, the profile's version 6 place and entry, OK resuming from 0:40, the reopen and a relaunch landing on it, S1 E3 watched at 4:50 landing on S2 E1 with the seasons' row on season 2); `show-details.spec.ts` (OK on an episode now plays it, and the reopen lands on it). `e2e:media` (`player-media.spec.ts` +1): S1 E1's MKV left a minute in resumes within 5 s inside its file, plays to "The episode has ended", whose "Back" lands on S1 E2. Screenshots `episode-player-*`, `episode-resume-*`, `player-episode-resumed-en`, `player-episode-ended-en` looked at.
- **Mutation:** `artifacts/mutate27c1.py`, 100 mutants written before the run (the profile's version 6, migration, `isShowProgress`, the shared cap, `withShow` and the edit; the app's reading and saving of a show's last episode; the episode's item, request, mark, play and places; the details' landing, `refresh`, OK and the rows' places; the player's episode and the show's key). Writing it first removed four guards that would have been equivalent mutants and closed three gaps (a show's last episode moving with no place written, in the profile and the app; a save time that is a string). 96 caught (`mutate27c1.log`): one equivalent (an unreadable stored entry kept as `null` reads back as `null`), three test gaps closed (the mark's number from the episode's place, OK on an unplayed episode further down, the season on show after a read with no episodes: the follower set it to -1), re-run 3/3 (`mutate27c1-b.log`). `artifacts/mutate27c1-e2e.py` 26/26 (the info bar's name and line, the end card under `e2e:media`, the row's place, text and bar, its CSS along the bottom, each piece of `main.tsx`'s wiring, both catalogs' mark, the end's words).
- **Size:** main's own code 389.0 → 392.8 KB (Tizen) and 376.3 → 380.1 KB (webOS); the query worker 114.7 → 115.4 KB (the profile's edit); app.css 87.7 → 88.6 KB; 219 core-js modules on both.
- **Verify:** locally lint, typecheck, unit 4,508, both TV builds, `pnpm e2e` 116/116, `e2e:media` 14/14, `verify:engines` on Chromium 53 and 68 16/16 (in `America/Phoenix` at 21:28; the walk's fixture is a playlist: no shows).
- **Merged:** PR #80 as merge commit `ce38653` on green CI (verify 9 m 18 s, engines 19 m 42 s, media 9 m 26 s on a rerun: the first tripped the 300 MB guide's declared-channels search budget, 20.2 ms of 20, a timing flake this step doesn't touch).

**Built in step 27c2: auto-next** (`apps/tv/src/shows/episode-play.ts`: `episodePlay`'s `seasons`, `place`, `kept` and `next`; `show-details.ts`'s `play(show, seasons, place, fromMs?)`; `player-state.ts`: `MoviePlay.next`, `UpNext`, `Player.upNext`, `NEXT_COUNTDOWN_S`, `NEXT_TICK_MS`, `startFilm`, the offer, the countdown and `playNext`; `PlayerScreen.tsx`: the end card's next line, countdown and "Play now", the screen's `data-next` and `data-countdown`; `player.css`: `.player__card-next`, `.player__card-countdown`, `.player__card-text`'s start side; the catalogs' `player.nextEpisode`, `player.nextIn`, `player.playNow`):
- **The next episode:** each episode's play knows the one after it (`MoviePlay.next`): the next in the panel's order across seasons (`nextPlace`), from where it was left, read when it is asked for; `null` after the show's last. The details hand `play` their seasons and the episode's place in them; `main.tsx` passes the places (`progress.point`) and the same `closed` (the details land again).
- **The card:** at an episode's end the player saves it watched, then asks for the next one. With one, the card says "The episode has ended", the show, the ended episode's line, then "Next: S2 E1 · Return of the Bars" (bold, one line, an ellipsis when long; «التالي: م2 ح1 · …») and "Starts in 10 s" («تبدأ خلال 10 ث»), with "Play now" (focused) and "Back". Without one (the show's last, or a movie), 27c1's card with "Back" alone. The card's lines sit on its start side, a provider's title in its own direction too (`.player__card-text`'s `text-align`, mirrored by rtlcss).
- **The countdown:** 10 s (`NEXT_COUNTDOWN_S`) in 1 s steps (`NEXT_TICK_MS`, the player's timers). Its end, or "Play now", plays the next episode in the same player (`playNext`: the card goes, the seek bar takes the keys, the info bar shows the new episode), from its place when it has one. "Back" (the button, the Back key or STOP) closes the player onto the details, which land on the next episode (the ended one is watched). A close drops the offer and its timer.
- **The app hiding** stops the countdown: the card stays without it, also once the app shows again; "Play now" and "Back" still work. An episode that ends while the app is hidden is offered without one.
- **Decided in leg 74 (not gates):**
  - **`MoviePlay.next` is a function, asked at the end:** the next one's place is read then (a place saved while the episode played counts), and a play builds the one after it only when needed. Each next play has its own `next`, so the chain runs to the show's last.
  - **The card keeps "The episode has ended" and the ended episode's line,** with the next one under them: the viewer sees what ended and what comes.
  - **The countdown doesn't come back when the app shows again:** a viewer back from elsewhere chooses ("Play now" or "Back").
  - **The next episode resumes from its place** like OK on its row: a viewer who left it part way meant to come back to it.
- **Tests:** 4,527 unit tests in the full local run (+19). `episode-play.test.ts` (the play from the seasons and a place: the episode at it, its length from its own place, the mark; the next across seasons, each from its place read when asked, `null` after the last, a next one's kept length); `show-details.test.ts` (each play handed the seasons on show and the episode's place); `player-state.test.ts` (+6: the countdown's constants; the offer after the watched save, "Play now" focused, the countdown second by second, the next one from its place in the same player with the card gone and the seek bar focused; the last episode and a play with no `next`; "Play now" at once and a failure after it ("Try again", Back onto the new play's details); "Back" by button and key, a dispose; the app hiding: the countdown gone for good, an end while hidden, a hide with no card). e2e `auto-next.spec.ts` (en-US, ar-SA): S2 E1 left at 0:30; S1 E3 played and ended by an `ended` event on the stage's `<video>` (Html5Engine passes it on; the mock's streams are held); the card's lines, their start side, the next line on one line, "Play now" drawn focused; the profile's S1 E3 watched; the countdown at 8; S2 E1 from 0:30 after at least 9 s, its line and length; the app hiding (`visibilitychange`) takes the countdown away for good; "Play now" plays S2 E2 at once; the last episode's card with "Back" alone, landing on S2 E2; S2 E1 again, its card's "Back" and the Back key landing on S2 E2. `e2e:media`: S1 E1's real end now shows the next line and countdown, and S1 E2 plays after it, its frames moving; Back lands on it. Screenshots `auto-next-*`, `auto-next-played-*`, `auto-next-last-*`, `player-episode-ended-en`, `player-episode-next-en` looked at (they found the Arabic card's English show name on the left).
- **Mutation:** `artifacts/mutate27c2.py`, 53 mutants written before the run (the play at its place, its next, the details' seasons and place, the offer, the card's buttons, the countdown, "Play now", the close, the app hiding). Writing it took out `onStatus`'s `else stopNext()` (it could never run) and a redundant `text-align`. 52 caught (`mutate27c2.log`); the survivor showed `playNext`'s own card reset redundant (the new play's `opening` takes the card away, as for "Try again"): taken out with its two mutants, re-run 51/51 (`mutate27c2-b.log`). `artifacts/mutate27c2-e2e.py`, 22 (the card's lines and buttons, the screen's data, the CSS, `main.tsx`'s places and place, both catalogs): 20 caught; the two survivors, the "Back" button's focus binding, were a test gap (the focus path comes from the screen's root): the spec now checks which button draws the focus, re-run 2/2.
- **Size:** main's own code 392.8 → 394.6 KB (Tizen) and 380.1 → 381.9 KB (webOS); the workers unchanged; app.css 88.6 → 89.3 KB; 219 core-js modules on both.
- **Verify:** locally lint, typecheck, unit 4,527, both TV builds, `pnpm e2e` 118/118, `e2e:media` 14/14, `verify:engines` on Chromium 53 and 68 16/16 (in `Pacific/Pitcairn` at 21:44; the walk's fixture is a playlist: no shows).
- **Merged:** PR #82 as merge commit `8c9840a` on green CI (verify 10 m 3 s, engines 18 m 28 s, media 12 m 2 s on a rerun: the first run tripped `test/epg-declared-300mb.media.test.ts`'s 60 ms store budget at 82.9 ms, the known slow-runner flake).

**Built in step 27d: "Continue watching"** (`packages/data/src`: `QueryService.itemsByKey`, `KeyItemsRequest`, `KEY_ITEMS_MAX`; the profile's `ShowProgress.more`; core's `keySource` (exported); `DataEngine.itemsByKey`; `apps/tv/src/movies`: `movie-progress.ts`'s save times, `unfinished` and the show's bar, `movies-state.ts`'s `continue` entry (`CONTINUE_MAX`, `heldEntry`, `applyContinued`, `refreshContinued`), `MoviesScreen.tsx`'s label; `episode-play.ts`'s `EpisodeMark.more`; `player-state.ts`'s save with it; the catalogs' `continue.title`):
- **The list** (`MovieProgress.unfinished`): what was left part way, newest first by the save times kept with the places and the shows (on an equal time by key): every place kept (a movie's, a playlist's episode's, and a panel's episodes', which no catalog lists and so never show up), and every show whose last episode isn't watched (resume it, or start it: a next episode stopped in its first 10 s keeps no place but still counts) or is watched with an episode after it. The order is a merge of halves: no `Array.prototype.sort` in the TV bundle (Babel would add core-js's, and Chromium 53-68's isn't stable).
- **"An episode after it"** is the show's entry's `more` (optional, no migration: the profile stays at version 6): the player says it with each save of an episode (`EpisodeMark.more`, from the details' seasons: `nextPlace`). An entry from before (no `more`) is taken as the show's last episode. A show finished to its last episode isn't listed again when its panel adds one: "Recently updated" lists it.
- **The items** (`QueryService.itemsByKey`): the query worker finds each key in its source's loaded search segment (the key map compares 53-bit hashes), reads only the chunks of the items found (one transaction for those not cached), and checks each item's own key, so a hash that only collides gives nothing. It answers in the order asked, of one kind, from the sources asked, the first `limit` found (at most 100). A removed source's key, an item its catalog no longer lists, or an episode's key finds nothing. No item is copied into the profile.
- **The rail:** "Continue watching" («متابعة المشاهدة», the same words on both screens) is the first entry of the movies rail and of the series rail when something there was left part way, before "Recently added" / "Recently updated", at most 20 (`CONTINUE_MAX`), and the screen opens on it. Its items are read with the categories (`progress.unfinished()` with the screen's kind and the stored sources; no read when nothing was left) and held like "Recently added"'s (no chunks; posters at the rest). A show's tile draws its last episode's bar (`share`: a watched one keeps no place, so none then). OK is the grid's: a movie's details open on "Resume from 0:40" (focused), a show's details on the episode to resume, or the next one once it was watched.
- **Back on top:** the details and the player close onto the screen; when a save changed the places since the entry was read (the places' `version`), it is read again at that focus change (`refreshContinued`). The entry comes, goes or takes the new list; the entry on show stays on show and the rail's place moves with the entries; in "Continue watching" the focus follows its item to its new place (the movie played again goes first), keeps its index when the item is gone, and the entry gone, the first entry from its first item. A load (open, catalog swap) drops a read still out; nothing is read on a failed or loading screen, nor while the screen is covered.
- **Decided in leg 75 (not gates):**
  - **A lookup by key in the query worker, not a copy of each item in the profile:** the search segments already map every key to its chunk, so a lookup costs a binary search per key and one read per chunk found. A copy would grow the profile (written every 10 s while playing) by a title, poster and URL per place (500 places), go stale (renamed, new poster, a new password in the URL), and still need the sources to drop a removed one.
  - **OK opens the details, not the player:** the details already land on "Resume" or on the episode to resume or the next one, and a watched show's next episode needs its seasons (`get_series_info`), which the details read. Two presses, the same on every tile.
  - **A show whose last episode was started and left within 10 s is listed** (from that episode's start): auto-next's countdown starts the next episode, and a viewer who stops it at once is still in the middle of the show.
  - **"More" saved with each episode, not asked of the panel when the list is read:** no request per watched show on every open.
  - **Read again only when the screen is back on top:** not on every save while the player plays over it.
  - **Parental control (step 38) must filter this list too**, as "Recently added".
- **Tests:** 4,549 unit tests in the full local run (+22). `query-service.test.ts` (+4: the items of the keys asked in their order from their chunks, of one kind, from the sources asked; a source not asked or not stored, an episode's key, an item gone, a broken key; the first `limit`, a key asked twice, the limit's bounds; the generation loaded until a reload, a key whose hash only collides; over RPC); `catalogs.test.ts` (+4: the new string); `profile.test.ts` (+1: `more` kept, compared, written only when said, never as `undefined`; anything but a boolean refused); `episode-play.test.ts` and `player-state.test.ts` (the mark's `more`, the save's `+next` / `last`); `movie-progress.test.ts` (+3: the list's order by save time and key, the shows going on, times that aren't numbers, a stored `more: false` saved again writing nothing, the show's bar; saves moving things to the front and taking them away, a watched show kept only with an episode after it; a merge of 37 shuffled times); `movies-state.test.ts` (+10: the entry first, the ask, its posters, OK and bars; none with no places or none found; the series screen's kind; coming back on top, the rail's place and the focus; the focus following its item, keeping its index, the entry gone; a screen of it alone; a read dropped by a load, a failed one, a catalog swap keeping the place, an answer after a close or a dispose; nothing read on a failed or loading screen; the posters of a new list when the focus keeps its place; an older answer dropped). e2e `continue-watching.spec.ts` (en-US, ar-SA: nothing played, "Recently added" first; Big Buck Bunny left at 0:40, back on the grid "Continue watching" first with "Recently added" on show; its tile's bar (6.7 %), "Continue watching · 1 movie"; OK on "Resume from 0:40", OK from 0:40; the same for S1 E2 in the series screen (13.3 %); S1 E2 watched with S1 E3 after it, the profile's `more`, the show staying with no bar, OK landing on S1 E3; a relaunch opening both screens on it); `movie-resume`, `movie-details`, `show-details` and `episode-resume` follow the rail's new first entry. Screenshots `continue-movies-*`, `continue-series-*` looked at.
- **Mutation:** `artifacts/mutate27d.py`, 83 mutants written before the run (the worker's limit, sources, kind, duplicates, chunk read and key check; the profile's `more`; the mark and the save; the times, the list and its merge, the show's bar; the screen's cap, read, rail, held list and refresh). Writing it took out seven things that could never fail a test (the show's bar skipping a watched episode, a stale time's delete, the shift guard, the list's reset on close and dispose, `gridAt >= 0`, a `Math.max`, the answer's status check, a `disposed` check on a warning) and added six tests. 80 caught (`mutate27d.log`): a second `Math.max` was redundant too (the navigator clamps the rail's place), taken out; two test gaps closed (an older refresh's answer after a newer one; a new list's posters when the focus keeps its place and the read answers after the rest), re-run 3/3 (`mutate27d-b.log`). `artifacts/mutate27d-e2e.py` 7/7 (the label, both catalogs, the engine's call, both screens' places and the save's `more` in `main.tsx`).
- **Size:** main's own code 394.6 → 397.1 KB (Tizen), 381.9 → 384.4 KB (webOS); the query worker 115.4 → 116.2 KB; 219 core-js modules on both.
- **Verify:** locally lint, typecheck, unit 4,549, both TV builds, `pnpm e2e` 120/120, `e2e:media` 14/14, `verify:engines` on Chromium 53 and 68 16/16 (the walk's fixture is a playlist: nothing played, no "Continue watching").

**Built in step 28a: the search screen** (step 28 was split in leg 76 into 28a, this, and 28b, Home. `apps/tv/src/search`: `search-state.ts` (`createSearch`, `SEARCH`, `SEARCH_VISIBLE`), `SearchScreen.tsx` (`kindText`, `groupText`, `searchHint`, `countText`, `SearchRow`), `search.css`; `DataEngine.find` and `items`; `live-state.ts`: `HEADER_SEARCH`, `openSearch`, `FoundChannel`, `playFound`, `focusRow` and `keepZone`; `LiveApp.tsx`'s header button and screen branch; `main.tsx`'s `select` and the refresh scheduler's `search.catalogChanged`; the catalogs' `search.*`):
- **The way in:** "Search" in the live header between "Series" and "Settings": Guide 0, Movies 1, Series 2, Search 3, Settings 4 (`HEADER_SEARCH`). It opens in every state of the live list (it reads its own data). The specs that reach "Settings" press once more (5 specs); the old-engine walk reaches only "Guide" and "Movies". The fifth button didn't fit in the pseudo-locale (text 40 % longer: "Settings" ended 4 px past the safe area even with the meta line at nothing), so the header's parts now sit 24 px apart (they were 48) and its buttons have 24 px sides (32 elsewhere); en-XA keeps about 20 px.
- **The screen** (`search`, pushed on the live navigator and painted instead of the live screen; the player and the details open over it): the header "Search" («البحث») with "{n} results", or "The best 200 of 5,432" when the list is cut, and a spinner while the first answer for the text is on its way; the field "Channels, movies and series" (OK opens the TV's keyboard through the text editor), its line "Part of a name, in any language", "Nothing has these words." when nothing was found, "Couldn't search your sources." on a failure; then the rows (88 px, 7 in view): the item's name (provider text, `dir=auto`, `translate=false`) over its kind and group ("Channel · Test News", «قناة · Test News»; a group without a name gets our "Other channels/movies/series").
- **A search per change of the text** (`DataEngine.find`: the query worker's `search` over the profile's sources in its order, at most 200 hits as the core ranks them, `total` counting every match): the search before is cancelled through the RPC's `CancelToken` (one still waiting never scans, and its answer is never sent back), and an answer for older text, an older opening or a closed screen is dropped. The last rows stay until the new answer; spaces search nothing. No debounce: TV keyboards type slowly, and the budget is per change.
- **The rows' items** (`DataEngine.items`: the hits' places read through the worker's 16-chunk cache): pages of 20 hits, only the window's and one window either side; a page read stays read for the answer on show, and a page that failed is asked again when the window moves.
- **OK on a row** whose item is read: a channel plays from its own group (`Live.playFound`: the rail shows its source's category, its own entry rather than "All channels"; its row is found from the hit's chunk, the category read on for an older generation's hit; the row is focused and played, so CH+/CH− zap through the group); a movie's details open; a show's details open (a playlist's episode: the movie details, `selectSeries`). Back from what opened returns to the same row.
- **Under the search, the live screen keeps its zone** (`keepZone`): Back from a player calls `focusRow`, which made the list active; while another screen is on top the zone now stays, so Back from the search lands on the header's "Search" and DOWN goes to the channel played. On top (the list's or the guide's player), `focusRow` still moves the focus to the row.
- **Back:** results → field → closed (the header's "Search"). **A catalog swap** (`catalogChanged`, from the refresh scheduler's `onCatalog`) searches the text again, keeping the focus's row; an empty answer moves the focus to the field.
- **Decided in leg 76 (not gates):** the header's place (Settings stays last, as on most TV apps); the TV's own keyboard (no on-screen keyboard in v1 unless step 16's TV visit finds the keyboards failing); one ranked list, no kind filter (later, if users ask: a query matching many channels can push the movies past 200); a search per change, no debounce; a channel played from its own group.
- **Parental control (step 38) must filter the results too**, as "Recently added" and "Continue watching".
- **At scale** (`e2e/search-throttled.spec.ts` in the `throttled` project, `e2e/big-catalog.ts`'s series): 200,000 items (30,000 channels, 150,000 movies, 20,000 series), 40 changes typed (Latin, Arabic, Backspaces) at 4x CPU, each timed from the key to its rows drawn: median 29 ms, max 63 ms locally, no long task. The CDP throttle doesn't slow dedicated workers, so the spec also times the worker's `search` and `items` calls and checks the 300 ms budget on the drawn time plus 3 times the worker's: median 69 ms, max 163 ms.

Home after "Series", so only the specs past it shift and the old-engine walk (Guide, Movies) doesn't. The icons are ours, inline SVG drawn in `currentColor` (Lablo Sans has no symbols), in an **icon-only `Button`** (hook-free): the icon alone in a square as tall as the button (56 px in the header), the label only as `aria-label`; without an icon the label stays. With six buttons the header keeps its en-XA margin (the pseudo-locale test now also checks the icons' names are catalog text).
- **The screen** (`home`, pushed on the live navigator and painted instead of the live screen, as the movies screen; the details and the player open over it): the header "Home" («الرئيسية») and the clock, then the shelves, each a title (44 px, 12 px above the tiles) over a row of poster tiles (`VirtualList`, 184 x 276 px, 24 px apart, 8 in view): "Continue watching", "Recently added movies" («أفلام أضيفت حديثًا»), "Recently updated series" («مسلسلات حُدّثت مؤخرًا»), at most 20 tiles each. Two shelves (332 px, 32 px apart) fit under the header: `top` moves the shelves as little as it can to keep the focused one in view. An empty shelf isn't shown; with nothing at all, one line ("What you watch and what your sources add shows up here."); a failed read, "Couldn't read your sources." and "Try again".
- **The data:** read at each opening. "Continue watching": `progress.unfinished()`'s keys, one `itemsByKey` per kind (`movie`, `series`: a panel's show or a playlist's episode) over the profile's sources, merged back into that order (`mergeByKeys`), at most 20 (`CONTINUE_MAX`); a key no catalog lists (a removed source's, a panel show's episode) drops out. The other two: the query worker's `recent` of each kind, at most 20 (a playlist's items have no date). Tiles draw their bars (`share`, a show's from its last episode) on every shelf, and an episode's "S01 E02" under its name, as on the poster screens. The posters (`posters.ts`, by tier) are asked once the view rests: the focused shelf's tiles in view first, then the other shelf's.

OK on a tile: a movie's details, a show's details (a playlist's episode: the movie details, `selectSeries`), as on their own screens; Back from them returns to the tile. Back on Home closes it onto the header's "Home".
- **Read again:** "Continue watching" alone when Home is back on top after a save changed the places (their `version`, as the poster screens' `refreshContinued`); every shelf on a catalog swap (`catalogChanged`). The focus stays on its item when its shelf still has it, else on its place (clamped); its shelf gone, the first shelf's first tile. A movie played from "Recently added movies" therefore comes back to its own tile, with "Continue watching" now above it.

Like the last channel, the items hold their source's login in their URL (unencrypted on the TV like the source's: ADR-013; the backup without logins strips it, step 32d4a).
