- **The pairing client** (`PairService`, served by the pair worker): `open(base)` makes a fresh X25519 key pair, checks that the link will fit a QR code (`qrFits`, before any request), posts `{tvPk}`, and answers the code, the server's `exp`, the link `<base>/p#c=<code>&k=<tvPk>`, its QR modules and the address to type (`<host[:port]>/p`); a new code forgets the session before it, also when it fails. `poll()` asks with the poll token: `waiting`; `ready` once, with the payload opened (`openPairPayload`), decoded as strict UTF-8 and parsed (`pairPayloadSchema`); `unreadable` when it doesn't open or parse; `ended` on 410, 404, 401 and 409 (or with no session); `wait` with `Retry-After`'s milliseconds on 429; `failed` otherwise (the session stays). The secret key is forgotten with the read, whatever it gave. `status(category)` posts once after `ready`; 200, 409, 404 and 401 end the session, a 429, a server error or no connection keep it so it can be sent again; an answer that comes after a newer code was made doesn't touch the newer one. Bodies are written by hand (no `JSON.stringify` in TV code), every request has 10 s for its whole answer (a late answer is cancelled when it comes) and a 65 KB cap; results and errors hold no URL or token. `fetchTransport` takes a method and a body for it.
- **The worker:** `pair.worker.ts` serves `pairHandlers` (`open`, `poll`, `status`, `close`); `DataEngine.pairing()` starts one per opening of the screen and its `close` ends it; it runs beside the refresh slot. The secret key, the network, `tweetnacl`'s box and the zod schemas stay out of main.js: the pair worker is 133.5 KB of its 150 KB budget (the config worker 133.8 KB), and main.js's own code grew 0.5 KB (440.0 KB Tizen, 427.3 KB webOS). `test/pair-worker-bundle.test.ts` runs the bundled worker in a realm without Chromium 53's missing APIs, through a whole pairing with a scripted phone.
- **Tests:** `qr.test.ts` 33, `pair-service.test.ts` 31, the engine's pairing in `test/tv-data-engine.test.ts`, the worker bundle, the lint entry points (`@lablo/core/check-qr`, `@lablo/data/pair.worker`); unit 5,862.
- **Mutation:** `artifacts/mutate31c1.py`, 152 written first (writing them closed eleven test gaps: a lone high surrogate before U+E000, tied masks, the largest payload against the cap, a broken byte inside a string, a failed code after a good one, a link too long for a QR code, a status answering after a new code, cancelling late answers, close before a read, a worker that dies, the status through the engine); 144 caught on the first run, four equivalents replaced and four gaps closed (U+0080 and U+0800, a scaled finder-like run followed by too little light, the handlers' status), 152/152 after.
- **Verified:** lint, typecheck, unit 5,862, `build:tv`, e2e `crypto-vectors.spec.ts`, `verify:engines --engine chrome53 --engine chrome68`: all 18 runs as expected, the vectors page 209/209 in page and worker on both.
- **Merged:** PR #118 as merge commit `d7f7c2e` on green CI on the first run (verify 14 m 51 s, its `pnpm e2e` 145 passed; engines 19 m 31 s: all 114 runs as expected, the vectors page 209/209 on Chromium 53, 56 and 68).
- **Not yet:** the screen that uses it (31c2: the poll, the renewal, the add path and the statuses) and its e2e (31c3).

**Built in step 31c2a: the TV's pairing screen and its add path** (`apps/tv/src/pairing/`: `pair-state.ts`, `pair-draft.ts`, `pair-qr.ts`, `PairPage.tsx`, `pair.css`; `onboarding/load-text.ts`, `form.ts`'s guide list, `engine-loader.ts`'s `typedGuides` and slot wait, `onboarding-state.ts`'s third row, `settings/sources-state.ts`'s header and `reload`, `settings-state.ts`, `main.tsx`'s `pairingOn`; the e2e's stand-in `tools/engines/src/pair-stand-in.ts`, `e2e/config-server.ts`, `e2e/pair-phone.ts`):
- **Where:** onboarding's source screen has a third row, "Use your phone", which pushes the `pair` screen on onboarding's navigator; once the phone's source is added, onboarding's done screen replaces it as the root. Settings › Sources has a header button, "Add from your phone" (as the guides screen's "Add a guide": the list keeps the first focus, an empty list gives it to the button), which pushes the same screen on the live navigator; its done view's "Done" returns to Sources, read again, and the live screens read the catalogs and guides again (`sourceAdded`).
- **One screen, its views** (`createPairing`): `opening` and `code` (Cancel), `unavailable` (no code: Try again; "too many codes" for a 429), `ended` (Show a new code), `loading` (the loader's progress: Cancel), `failed` and `refused` (Show a new code, Try again), `done` (Done). Each opening starts a pair worker (`DataEngine.pairing()`); it ends when the screen leaves the stack (Back, Cancel, Done, onboarding's reset) or with `dispose`, after a status still being sent. A worker whose calls reject is ended, and "Try again" starts another.
- **The poll:** every `PAIR_POLL_MS` (2 s) while a code shows; `wait` as `Retry-After` says; after a `failed` poll the code view says "Can't reach Lablo's server. Still trying…" until one answers. Stopped while the app is hidden; asked at once when it shows again (not twice when a poll is running or due). A code the poll says ended is renewed (the TV's clock can't be held against the server's `exp`); after three codes ended unused the screen waits for "Show a new code". A payload that doesn't open or parse, one the TV form would refuse, and an M3U file (until 31d) give a new code with a note ("What your phone sent couldn't be read…") and no status: only a payload not from the phone page can be one.
- **The add path:** `pairDraft` reads the payload through the TV form's own `checkForm` (the server's base or the link normalized, the login trimmed, the name or the host) and each guide link through `normalizeLink`, at most four, one given twice kept once. Then the config's `block-new-sources` (refused: the rule's words, status `blocked`), then the engine loader, as onboarding's "Add source" (`DataEngine.refresh`: the blocklist, the provider's answer). `SourceDraft.epgUrl` became `epgUrls`; `typedGuides` names a source's guides `<id>`, `<id>-2`, `<id>-3`, `<id>-4` by their place (ids are never reused, as source ids aren't). The loader now waits for the refresh slot (`refreshing`, every 500 ms, right before each refresh) instead of failing on a background refresh, and Settings stops the background refreshes while a paired source loads (`busy`), starting them again after (unless the gate stopped them meanwhile).
- **The status** (`statusFor`): `ok` once the catalog is saved (the loader's `guide` phase, or its end when it never gets there); `auth` → `auth_failed`; `expired` and the panel's `blocked` → `expired`; `blocklisted` and the config's refusal → `blocked`; the rest → `unreachable`. One per payload ("Try again" sends none; the phone shows the TV's first try), tried up to three times, 2 s then 4 s apart; a load cancelled on the TV sends nothing.
- **The screen** (`PairPage.tsx`): on the start side the steps (scan the QR code; or open the address, shown `dir="ltr"`; then type the code), the code in two groups of four in the accent colour (`dir="ltr"`, never translated), the state in words and the note; on the end side the QR code and the privacy line ("Your details are encrypted on your phone. Only this TV can read them."). The QR is one SVG `<path>` of the dark modules (`pair-qr.ts`: a rectangle per run) on a white square with a 4-module quiet zone, whole pixels per module (`qrModulePx`: 10 px for a version 5 code, 450 px a side), `shape-rendering="crispEdges"`; rtlcss mirrors the layout and never the drawing. The loading, failed and done views use onboarding's words (`loading.*`, `error.*`, `done.*`, moved to `load-text.ts`). Seventeen new catalog keys in en and ar (`pair.*`, `onboarding.phone`, `onboarding.phoneHint`, `sources.addPhone`).
- **Size:** main.js's own code grew 15.4 KB (455.4 KB Tizen, 442.7 KB webOS, of 500): the state about 4.6 KB, the page 4.1 KB, the catalogs' new strings 3.5 KB, the wiring 1.5 KB, measured in the minified bundle. The new files' core-js modules (`artifacts/babel-polys30b2.ts`): `es.array.push`, and the page's JSX spread helpers (`es.object.get-own-property-descriptors`, `web.dom-collections.for-each`), already in main. The pair worker is unchanged (133.5 KB).
- **E2E** (`e2e/pairing.spec.ts`, 3 tests in `pnpm e2e`): the test's config server answers the pairing routes from a stand-in in memory (`PairStandIn`: 31a's answers without the database, the rate limits or files; the TV's CORS and preflight), and a scripted phone in the test process takes the typed-code path (`GET /v1/pair/:code`, the payload sealed with `@lablo/crypto`, then the progress). (1) Onboarding by phone in English: the code shown and polled, the QR a version 5 code at 450 px, an M3U link from the mock with the same guide link twice; the guide held so the loading view stays in its guide phase while the phone already reads `ok`; the done screen, the source and one guide (`s1`) kept, and none of the login, the links or the name in anything the stand-in kept. (2) Settings › Sources in Arabic: the header button, the code view mirrored with the QR on the left and the code left to right; a wrong password → the failed view (`auth`), `auth_failed` on the phone, nothing kept; "Show a new code", an M3U link with an Arabic name, the done view, and Sources read again with both. (3) A code ended on the server is renewed; the config's `block-new-sources` then refuses the phone's source (`blocked`), nothing kept, Back to the source screen. Screenshots `pair-{code,loading,refused}-en`, `pair-{sources,code,failed,done}-ar`, looked at.
- **Tests:** `pair-state.test.ts`, `pair-draft.test.ts`, `pair-qr.test.ts`, `pair-page.test.ts` (84 between them), the guide list and the slot wait in `form.test.ts` and `engine-loader.test.ts`, onboarding's third row, the Sources header and reload, Settings' wiring, the catalogs' keys; unit 6,029.
- **Mutation:** `artifacts/mutate31c2a.py`, 117 written first (writing them closed five test gaps: the constants used only through their names, a relaunch alone, "visible" while a poll was due, the page's words per view and failure, a Sources reload whose two reads gave the same answer); 112 caught on the first run. Three gaps closed and caught on a rerun: the focus reset on a view change (a rule arriving with "Try again" focused), an Xtream payload's guide links, and the slot wait's cancel check (a cancelled load's wait that kept sleeping was unseen). Two equivalents kept with their reasons: Back's `cancelLoad` (the pop's subscriber closes the screen, cancelling the load, before any answer) and `poll()`'s view check (a poll timer exists only while a code shows). 115/117 caught, 2 equivalent.
- **Verified:** lint, typecheck, unit 6,029, `build:tv` (sizes above), e2e `onboarding`, `config`, `config-blocklist`, `config-modes`, `config-gate` and `pairing` (28/28), `verify:engines --engine chrome53 --engine chrome68`: all 18 runs as expected (the onboarding walks pass with the source screen's third row).
- **Merged:** PR #119 as merge commit `c015e7a` on green CI: engines 21 m 19 s (all 114 runs as expected on Chromium 53, 56 and 68, 83 seeded failures caught); verify green on a rerun (12 m 5 s, its `pnpm e2e` 148 passed). The first verify failed once in `e2e/auto-next.spec.ts` (ar-SA, unrelated): `endEpisode` dispatched `ended` on the stage's `<video>` while the player was still `opening` the next play (`data-plays` 6), and the event was lost.

**Built in step 31c2b: the pairing screen on the old engines** (`tools/engines/src`: `pairing.ts`, `pair-server.ts`, `pair-phone.ts` (moved from `e2e/`), `docker.ts`'s `forwardToHost`, `variants.ts`'s `pairing` screen and `PAIR_SEEDS`; `verify-engines.ts`; `e2e/config-server.ts` answers through `answerPair`; docs/runbooks/old-engines.md item 7):
- **How the engines reach the stand-in:** the harness runs `PairStandIn` (31c2a's) in a Node server on the host (`startPairServer`, on `127.0.0.1` with Docker Desktop and `0.0.0.0` on Linux, as the lab's collector). A TV build takes `?api=` only for a host on the device (`readApiBase`), so `host.docker.internal` can't be given to it; `forwardToHost` runs the image's own socat in each engine's container (`docker exec --detach`), from its `127.0.0.1:8091` to the host's port, and the app is opened with `?api=http://127.0.0.1:8091`. No image change (the apt layer stays untouched), no new dependency.
- **The walk** (`runPairing`, a screen of the `app` variant on both bundles, after onboarding): in English and in Arabic from a fresh database, the language, the disclaimer, "Allow", ArrowDown twice to "Use your phone", OK; the code view; the scripted phone (`pair-phone.ts`, now shared by the e2e and the harness) posts the fixture playlist's link and a name sealed to the TV's key; onboarding's done screen. Its probe (`PAIR_EXPRESSION`, ES5) reads the pair screen's data attributes, texts, the QR's `<path>` and every part's box.
- **The checks:** the code in two groups of four and the address; the QR code module by module against `encodeQr` of the link the TV must hold (`<api>/p#c=<code>&k=<tvPk>`, the key as the stand-in got it), read back from the path's runs (`drawnModules`: nothing but `qrPath`'s runs, each run back the way it went); whole pixels per module (`qrSidePx`), `crispEdges`, no transform; the QR on the end side; every part inside the title-safe area; the phone reading `done` with `ok`; the profile holding the one source under the phone's name with the walk's language and consent; no uncaught error.
- **The seed** (`seed-pair-qr-mirrored`, in `--self-test`): a style that flips the QR code (`.pair__qr { transform: scaleX(-1); }`). It must fail on every engine; on Chromium 53 it fails on the transform in both walks and both bundles (the drawing itself is still right, so only the transform check can catch it).
- **Results:** Chromium 53 and 68, both bundles, both languages, every check on the first run: the code 0.2 s after OK, the playlist added 3.4–3.9 s (53) and 2.2–2.3 s (68) after the phone's send, a version 5 QR code at 450 px. `--self-test` on 53: 42 runs as expected, 31 seeded failures caught. Screenshots `pairing-<engine>-<platform>-<en|ar>-<code|done>.png` (CI uploads them) looked at on 53.

**Built in step 31c3: the TV's pairing against the real API** (`e2e/pairing-api.spec.ts`, `e2e/api-front.ts`, `e2e/pairing-steps.ts`; `playwright.web.config.ts`'s `tv` project and second web server; docs/runbooks/web.md "The TV's pairing against the API"):
- **What runs:** `pnpm e2e:web` (CI's `web` job) starts `next start` (127.0.0.1:3101, a fresh `lablo_e2e`) and the TV's Vite dev app (`localhost:5393`); the spec runs the mock provider in its process, the scripted phone (`tools/engines/src/pair-phone.ts`, the typed-code path) over HTTP, and the TV's onboarding and Settings by remote keys.
- **The test front** (`startApiFront`): the TV's `?api=` is a pass-through before `next start` on 127.0.0.1. Requests go on unchanged and answers come back unchanged, without the connection headers (`HOP_HEADERS`; `host` is each hop's own), so the TV meets the real routes' CORS and preflights from another origin, as a TV build does. It sets each test's own `x-forwarded-for` (the rate limits are per address; no `LABLO_RATE_SECRET` is needed with one process). `GET /v1/config` reaches the real route (the committed, dev-signed config, which the dev app trusts) unless a test serves its own, since the route bundles the committed file at build time. Rejected: a runtime config override in the web app (test machinery in production code), and the stand-in (the pairing routes must be the real ones). The front logs every request it passed on with the API's status (`forwarded`).
- **Found while building:** the real route's `cache-control: private, max-age=300` keeps a config refresh within 5 minutes in the browser's cache (as intended on a TV), so a test that serves a config serves it before the TV's first request.
- **The cases (5):** onboarding by phone with an Xtream login (the phone reads `open`, `received` while the mock's API is held, then `ok`; the live list fills); Settings › Sources by phone in Arabic with an M3U link and a guide link (`ok`, Sources lists two, the guide named `s2`); one screen, a new code each time: a wrong password (`auth_failed`), the mock's `expired` account (`expired`), a closed port (`unreachable`); a served blocklist holding the mock's host (`blocked`, in Arabic); a served `block-new-sources` (`blocked`, the rule's message). Each checks the session's row in `lablo_e2e` (no payload left, the read time and the status set), the profile the TV kept (nothing after a refusal), every preflight answered 204 by the API, and no error on the page. Screenshots `pair-api-{code-en,code-ar,done-ar,unreachable-en,blocklisted-ar}` looked at.
- **CI:** the `web` job's path filter also takes the TV app, the packages' sources, the mock and the spec's own files; it uploads `pair-api-*.png` with the phone page's screenshots. The root config's `chromium` project ignores the spec (`pnpm e2e` has no database).
- **Tests:** `test/api-front.test.ts` 13 (the headers both ways, a request passed on whole, preflights and statuses, its own connection header, the config answer set and cleared, a dead API, an API dying halfway, a browser giving up, a close with a poll on its way). `pairing.spec.ts` (31c2a) uses the shared steps.

**Built in step 31d1: the M3U file's way up and down** (`@lablo/protocol`'s `presignedUrlSchema`; `apps/web/src/server/dev-blob.ts` and the route `src/app/dev/blob/[...key]/route.ts`; `blob-store.ts`'s `DEV_BLOB_PATH`; `http.ts`'s `readBytes`; the phone page's file form in `src/phone/`; docs/runbooks/web.md "The M3U file"):
- **Development URLs are paths on the API's own origin** (the decision RELAY step 31d left open): the memory store presigns `/dev/blob/<key>?m=…&n=…&e=…&s=…`, which the phone page and the TV resolve against the address they reach the API at. The page, the API and the development blobs are then one origin, so a phone on the LAN needs nothing set, the page's `connect-src 'self'` holds without a development exception, and 31e's test front sees the file's bytes pass like every other request. The upload and file answers' schema takes an `https://` URL (R2's) or exactly that path (`/dev/blob/`, the blob key's characters, `?`, the query's characters: no other host, no dots, nothing escaped); a relative path can only name the API's origin, and the route answers 404 on Vercel. `LABLO_DEV_BLOB_URL` is gone. Turned down: `http://` for loopback hosts only, which needs an address per machine and port, fails a LAN phone, and meets a page served through 31e's front as another origin.
- **The development route** `/dev/blob/[...key]`: `PUT` and `GET` through `MemoryBlobStore.redeem` with the request's path and query, as R2 answers its own URLs (403 a changed, expired or other-method URL; 400 another length; 404 nothing there). The `PUT`'s body is read with a cap of the URL's own size (at most `PAIR_FILE_MAX_BYTES`; a size the URL can't name reads nothing), so a body past it is refused without being read whole; the URL is still checked first. The `GET` answers `application/octet-stream` to any origin (`access-control-allow-origin: *`, also on its refusals: the TV is another origin); the `PUT` has no CORS headers (the page is the same origin). Every answer is `no-store` with no body but the file. 404 on Vercel before the services are asked, and whenever the services' store isn't the memory one (step 34's R2 adapter serves its own URLs).
- **The phone's file form:** a third source type, "M3U playlist file". The input stays in the page, focusable but unseen (a browser's own "No file chosen" is in the browser's language), its label is the button ("Choose a file", then "Choose another file"), and the chosen file's name and size show under it (`fileSize`: kilobytes under a megabyte, else megabytes to one decimal). Refused on the field: no file, an empty file, one over 41,942,400 bytes (`FILE_MAX_BYTES`: 40 frames and their tags make the upload's 40 MiB). A file left unnamed names its source after itself, without `.m3u`/`.m3u8` and cut to 80 (`nameFromFile`). The file stays chosen through "Use another code" and "Fix and send again", and goes with "Add another source".
- **Sending a file** (`flows.ts`): the file read one 1 MiB frame at a time (`File.slice`), each frame `sealFileFrame`d under a fresh `fileKey` and nonce base, the frames joined into one `Blob` (no whole plain copy); then `uploadUrl(code, fileCipherLength(size))`, the `PUT` of exactly that length, then the payload `{v: 1, source: {type: "m3u-file", file: {key, fileKey, base, size}}, name?, epg?}` through `pairPayloadSchema` (`withFile`), sealed to the TV's key and posted as before. The frames are sealed before the upload URL is asked for, so a file that can't be read costs no code guess: "This file couldn't be read. Choose it again." (the choice is forgotten). A code that ended, too many tries, or an upload that fails each end the send as a payload's failure would (the form and the file kept; sending again asks for a new URL, which replaces the file). The button says "Encrypting and sending the file…" meanwhile. `api.ts` `PUT`s only to a URL `presignedUrlSchema` takes, and counts anything but a 2xx as no answer. tweetnacl's `secretbox` runs at about 160 MiB/s in Node on this PC, so a 40 MB file is well under a second's work on a phone.
- **For step 34:** R2's host joins the phone page's `connect-src`, and the R2 bucket's CORS rule allows a `PUT` from the page's origin and a `GET` from any.
- **Tests:** unit 6,148 (+76): `apps/web/test/dev-blob.test.ts` 10 (the store's relative URLs and the schema taking them; the route's PUT then GET, R2's codes, a body past the cap left unread, every size the URL can't name, the largest upload, Vercel and a store that isn't the memory one, other methods, the route module itself; `readBytes`), `phone-flows` +19 (the two calls, any PUT answer but a 2xx refused, a URL the schema refuses never fetched; the file's frames read in place, uploaded before the payload and opening with its keys to the file, new keys per send, each failure's view with the file kept, an unreadable or shrunk file asking for nothing; `encryptFile`, `pickedFile`), `phone-state` +5, `phone-code-form` +17 (each file problem and its limit, the default name, `withFile`), `phone-catalog` +25 (`fileSize` 12, the 13 new keys in both languages), `blob-store`, `pair-unit`, the protocol's 8 new samples; `test:web` 45 unchanged. e2e `apps/web/e2e/phone-file.spec.ts` 3 (in `pnpm e2e:web`): a file of three frames by QR link in English (the upload between the upload URL and the payload, the scripted TV downloading it from the API's origin with CORS, the stored bytes holding none of the file's text, `openFile` to exactly the file, 404 once the TV reports); a typed code in Arabic with a name, refused by the TV, then sent again with a new code under new keys; no file and an empty file refused with nothing asked.
- **Mutation:** `artifacts/mutate31d1.py`, 97 written first (writing them closed four gaps: a refused GET's CORS header, sizes the route can't use, a file that shrank, spaces left by a cut pair): 91 caught; four gaps closed after (a `//` path sample that also had dots, a 0-byte URL with a longer body, leading spaces before the 80-character cut, rounding to a tenth against rounding up) and two equivalents simplified (only a 200 has a body; only the file form has a file problem): 97/97.

**Built in step 31d2a: the file's bytes on the TV** (`packages/data/src/file-frames.ts`, `file-feed.ts`; `pair-service.ts`'s `file`; `ingest.ts`'s `m3u-file` source; `ingest-service.ts`; `apps/tv/src/data/engine.ts`'s `PairSession.file` and `RefreshOptions.file`):
- **The frames open in the pair worker** (RELAY step 31d2's decision, measured): `build:tv` with `openFileFrame` in the ingest worker made it 162.2 KB of its 150 KB (tweetnacl is kept whole, +32.8 KB), and the budget is ADR-003's. The pair worker holds tweetnacl already and runs for as long as the pairing screen shows, which covers the add. Its plain frames reach the ingest worker through a `MessageChannel` the app makes (`PairSession.file`): one port goes to the pair worker, the other, as a `FileFeed`, to the refresh (`RefreshOptions.file`), which transfers it with the ingest worker's `run`. The ingest pulls one frame at a time (`read` over RPC on the port), each frame's buffer transferred, never copied through main: at most one frame (1 MiB) is in flight.
- **Opening** (`openFileFrames`): a `ByteSource` of the plain file over the download's. Frame i's exact ciphertext (1 MiB and its 16-byte tag, the last one shorter) is gathered across network chunks and opened with `openFileFrame(…, i)` (nonce base plus i). A frame that doesn't open, a download that ends early, and a byte past the last frame (checked by one more read) each fail with `FileError` (code `file`); the connection's own failures pass through as `NetworkError`; a failure ends the reader for good and lets the download go.
- **The file call** (`PairService.file(port)`): only after a payload with an M3U file was read (the session keeps its `file`). `GET <base>/v1/pair/s/<sid>/file` with the poll token, in the time limit; 404 is `gone` (no file came), 429 `rate`, any other status `status`, an answer the schema refuses `malformed`, a URL naming another file than the payload's `file`. A path (development's `/dev/blob/…`) is resolved against the session's base, an `https://` URL used as it is. Its `GET` carries no token; its headers come in the time limit; 403 or 404 (expired or deleted) and a `content-length` other than `fileCipherLength(size)` are `file`. Then `read` and `cancel` are served on the port. A second call, a new code, the status and `close` end the feed (its server closed, its download let go), and a call one of them overtook answers `gone`. Results and errors never hold a URL or a key.
- **The feed** (`feedResponse`): the ingest worker's side, a `ByteResponse` with status 200 and `content-length` the plain size (the progress bar's total); a frame the pair worker couldn't open fails the read with `IngestError('file')`, any other failure there with `NetworkError`; `cancel` tells the pair worker.
- **The ingest:** `IngestSource` gains `{type: 'm3u-file'}` (`LinkSource` names the others: what has a catalog server). Its bytes come as `options.file` and are read through the M3U parser exactly as a link's body: no validators, no panel login, the byte hash still makes the same file `unchanged`. Without a feed, `IngestError('file')` before anything is written. `IngestErrorCode` gains `file`. A feed left part way by any failure is let go in the `finally`.
- **The data engine:** a file's refresh asks the blocklist nothing (a file has no catalog server; its streams meet the blocklist in `PlayerController.play`); `sourceUrlOf` takes a `LinkSource`.
- **Ending order:** the add's load is cancelled before its pair worker ends (the pairing screen's `close` runs `cancelLoad` before `endSession`); an ingest reading a feed whose worker is gone would wait until it is cancelled.
- **Sizes** (`build:tv`): the ingest worker 131.9 KB (+2.5), the pair worker 137.4 KB (+3.8), main.js 455.7 / 443.0 KB (+0.3). No new core-js module: the typed-array ones `openFileFrames` asks for come with `@lablo/crypto` already.
- **Tests:** unit 6,189 (+41): `file-frames.test.ts` 12 (real `sealFile` frames under every split: one chunk, 4 KB, frame-sized, across frames, one-byte chunks at the edges, empty chunks, 25 seeded random splits; files of one byte, one frame and a frame and a byte; a cut at no bytes, at a frame's edge, inside a frame and one byte short; two frames swapped and one dropped; a byte changed in a frame, a tag and the last frame; a byte past the end; another key or nonce base; the size one off either way; a network failure passed through and kept; cancel; one frame read ahead at most), `file-feed.test.ts` 6 (over a real `MessageChannel`: the frames' buffers transferred, the `file` and network mappings, cancel reaching the other side, a waiting read cancelled), `pair-service.test.ts` +13 (`file`: both requests and their headers, a path and an https URL, each failure of either request, both time limits and a late answer let go, no file payload, overtaken calls, the feed ended by a second call, the status (sent or not), `close` and its reader, a silent port after, the RPC handler), `ingest.test.ts` +6 (a three-frame file to a catalog with no request and its header's guides; the same file `unchanged`; a changed, cut or longer file `file` with the catalog kept; no feed; cancel; a file let go after a failure), `tv-data-engine.test.ts` +2 (a scripted phone's file through both workers to a catalog, no blocklist asked; a broken file; no feed; a file the pair worker couldn't get), `pair-worker-bundle.test.ts` +1 and `ingest-worker-bundle.test.ts` +1 (the bundled workers in the Chromium 53 realm: the file's URL, GET and frames, a nonce base of all 0xff; the mock's playlist ingested from a transferred port).
- **Mutation:** `artifacts/mutate31d2a.py`, 83 written first (writing them closed two gaps: an ended feed's server answering on, a file's refresh asking the blocklist; and simplified three equivalent branches): 81 caught; one gap closed after (the status's own `endFeed`, seen only when the status doesn't go), caught on the rerun; one equivalent kept (the cancel check after the playlist's last chunk: `take`'s checkpoint throws first): 82/82.
- **Verified locally:** lint, typecheck, unit 6,189, `build:tv` (the sizes above), `pnpm e2e` 148, `pnpm e2e:web` 15/15, `verify:engines --engine chrome53 --engine chrome68` all 22 runs as expected (onboarding and pairing walks, vectors 217/217).

**Built in step 31d2b1: the stored file source and its add path** (`packages/data/src/profile.ts`, `account-check.ts`; `apps/tv/src/onboarding/engine-loader.ts`, `form.ts`, `loader.ts`, `load-text.ts`; `apps/tv/src/pairing/pair-draft.ts`, `pair-state.ts`, `PairPage.tsx`; `data/refresh-scheduler.ts`, `settings/sources-state.ts`, `SettingsScreen.tsx`, `live/live-state.ts`, `movies/movies-state.ts`; RELAY step 31d2b's decisions):
- **Profile version 8:** `SourceRecord` gains `{id, type: 'm3u-file', name, added}`, a source with no link. The 7 → 8 migration changes only `v`: an app at version 7 would read a file source as a link with no URL, and an app never saves over a newer document (step 32's restore can refuse one). Versions 4–7 keep the two-variant `SourceRecordV7`. `sourceAccount` is `null` for a file (no panel to ask about the account).
- **The draft carries the feed:** `SourceDraft` gains `{type: 'm3u-file', name, epgUrls, file}`; `file()` fetches the frames in the pair worker (`PairSession.file`) and resolves the `FileFeed`, or rejects with a `LoadError` (`fileError`: `gone` and `file` are `file`, every other failure `network`). The onboarding form never makes one (`checkForm` gives a `FormDraft`).
- **The loader:** the slot free, then `file()`, then the slot again (normally no wait), then `refresh({src, source: {type: 'm3u-file'}}, {file})` at once: the refresh claims the slot with nothing awaited in between, and the blob's connection is never held through a long wait. A cancel after the feed came asks for no refresh; the feed is let go with the screen's close (the pair worker ends) or the status (the pair service ends it). Errors: the ingest's `file` (a frame that doesn't open) and the feed's own pass through as `LoadError('file')`.
- **Sent again replaces:** a file whose name is a stored file source's (exactly) loads under that source's id and keeps its `added`: the ingest replaces the catalog under the same ContentKeys, so favourites, places and the rail's choices carry over, and the `source` edit replaces the record in place. Its guide links the list doesn't have yet get `g<n>` ids past every listed and stored guide's (`freshGuides`, `nextGuideId` and `storedGuideIds`, now shared with the guides screen), never `<id>`, `<id>-2`…, which may name a guide the user removed whose records are still stored. Another name adds a source.
- **Everything that reads a stored source:** `ingestSourceOf` is `null` for a file and the schedule never refreshes it (only sending it again does; its guides are read with the others); `catalogOf` is a playlist's (its header names its guides); the live screen asks no short EPG for its channels; movies' playlists are every source but Xtream; Settings › Sources shows "M3U file" with no server, never blocked (its URL is empty, and the row says `false` whatever the answer), and its line "Sent from your phone {day}, {time}".
- **The pairing screen:** `pairDraft(payload, parts)` makes the file draft (the name trimmed, else `pair.fileName` in the page's language, read when the payload comes; the guide links checked as before) with the fetch on the session the payload came on. `statusFor('file')` is `unreachable`. A failed or refused file offers only "Show a new code" (`pairActions(kind, file)`): its status deletes the blob, so "Try again" could only fail. Its failures have a file's words: `error.file` ("The file from your phone couldn't be read. Send it again."), `error.fileFormat`, `error.fileNetwork` (en, ar).
- **Sizes** (`build:tv`): main.js 458.7 / 446.0 KB (+3.0 / +3.0 KB: the loader's file path, the page's file views, six strings in two languages); the workers unchanged. No new core-js module (`trim` and `Promise` were in main.js already).
- **Tests:** unit 6,254 (+65): `profile.test.ts` (version 8 pinned, the 7 → 8 migration, a file source stored and read back, the newer-version pins at 9), `engine-loader.test.ts` +13 (`nextGuideId`, `sameFile`, `freshGuides`, `storedGuideIds`, `ingestSourceOf` and `catalogOf` for a file; the file draft's order slot · file · slot · refresh; no file for a link; the second slot wait; the feed's failures; the ingest's `file`; a cancel during the feed and during the stored guides' read; sent again: the same id and `added`, `g<n>` past a stored id; another name or a link's name: a new id), `pair-draft.test.ts` (the file draft, its default name, a refused guide link, `fileError` for each failure, `statusFor('file')`), `pair-state.test.ts` +6 (the draft's fetch on its session, its failures' errors, the default name read per payload, a failed file's one button, a refused file's one button and a link's two after it), `pair-page.test.ts` +2, `sources-screen.test.ts` +4, `refresh-scheduler.test.ts` +1, `live-state.test.ts` +1, `movies-state.test.ts` +1, `account-check.test.ts`, and `tv-data-engine.test.ts` +1: the real loader and data engine add a two-frame file from a scripted phone (`v: 8`, the record with no link) and replace it under the same name (one source, its catalog 50 then 30 channels).

**Built in step 31d2b2a: the file by phone against the stand-in, and on the old engines** (`tools/engines/src`: `pair-stand-in.ts`, `pair-server.ts`, `pair-phone.ts`, `pairing.ts`; `verify-engines.ts`; `e2e/config-server.ts`, `e2e/pairing.spec.ts`; `packages/data/src/file-frames.ts`; `apps/tv/src/i18n/ar.ts`'s `done.body`; RELAY step 31d2b2's decisions; runbook `old-engines.md` item 7):
- **The stand-in learns the file** as the real routes answer it: `POST /v1/pair/:code/upload-url` (`{size}`, while the code works and nothing was sent; asking again deletes the earlier file; 404 for an unknown or ended code, 409 once a payload came, a sent code included after it ends), the blob, `GET /v1/pair/s/:sid/file` (the poll token; 409 before the payload's read, 404 with no file), and the file deleted with the status. Its bodies are bytes now (`answer(…, body: Uint8Array)`), and `standInPath` routes `/v1/pair…` and `/dev/blob/…` to it in both servers.
- **The blob uses development's URL shape** (`/dev/blob/pair/<sid>/<fileId>?m=PUT|GET&n=<size>&e=<exp>&s=<token>`), a path on the stand-in's origin, so the TV resolves it against its API base exactly as against `next start`: the protocol's schema takes only `https://` or that path. A URL is honoured only exactly as given and before `e` (403); a PUT takes exactly `n` bytes (400); a GET answers the bytes with CORS `*` and their length, then 404 once the status deleted them. The tokens are random and kept in a map (no HMAC). The test reads `blobs` and each session's `uploads` (every file uploaded, kept for it: the real API deletes them).
- **The scripted phone sends a file** as the phone page does (`phoneSendsFile`): the TV's key, the frames sealed under a new key and nonce base (`sealFile`), the upload URL for exactly `fileCipherLength`, the PUT (a path resolved against the origin), then the payload with the file's key, frame key, nonce base and length. `broken` flips one bit of the last frame after sealing, so the frames before it open and reach the ingest worker before the last fails its check. `phonePadded` pads a playlist at its end with 1 KiB `#PAD` comment lines to exactly the frames asked.
- **`e2e/pairing.spec.ts`** (`pnpm e2e`): in onboarding in English and from Settings in Arabic, a broken file first: the pairing screen's failed view with the file's own error (`data-code` `file`), the phone reading `unreachable`, one button ("Show a new code"), nothing kept and no file left on the stand-in; then the mock's playlist padded to three frames, its guide held for the loading view, added as an `m3u-file` source with no link; Sources' row "M3U file" with no server and "Sent from your phone {day}, {time}"; the uploads holding none of the playlist's text. In Arabic the phone sends no name, so the source takes `pair.fileName` in Arabic; the file names the mock's guide with a query of its own (the Xtream source added first already read the plain one, so its guide would not be read again, and the load would not stop in its guide phase).
- **The old-engine pairing walk:** the English walk keeps the playlist's link; the Arabic walk sends the staged plain playlist as a file of three frames (`PHONE_SENDS`, `FILE_FRAMES`, `PairingOptions.playlist`). So each engine and platform proves both ways in about the walk's old time, and the frames cross the `MessageChannel` between the pair worker and the ingest worker on the real old engine. `evaluatePairWalk` wants `s1,m3u-file,قائمة الهاتف` for the file and no file left on the stand-in (`PairWalk.blobs`); the report says how each walk sent (`sent`).
- **The walk found a bug on Chromium 53, fixed here:** a message posted on a `MessagePort` with an `ArrayBuffer` in its transfer list arrives there as `null` (a page with two workers on one channel showed it: copied frames and small messages arrive; Chromium 56 and 68 deliver the transfer too; a worker's own `postMessage` is not affected). `serveFileFrames` transferred each frame's buffer, so on 53 the ingest worker never got a frame and the load stayed in its first phase. The frames are copied now (`read: () => frames.read()`, a few milliseconds per 1 MiB frame), and `file-feed.test.ts` reads a whole file through a port that drops such a message, as 53 does. The walk also counted every file the stand-in held, so 53's failed walks failed 68's: `heldFiles` counts the walk's own session's.
- **Arabic `done.body`** is now «تمت إضافة {name}.» ("{name} was added"): the old «{name} جاهز.» is masculine, which read wrong after a feminine name such as the file's default «قائمة من هاتفك» (the pairing screen's done view and onboarding's done screen).

None appears.

**Built in step 32a: the backup API** (`apps/web/src/server/backup.ts`, `src/app/v1/backup/[lookupId]/route.ts`, `preflight`'s headers in `http.ts`, `@lablo/protocol`'s `BACKUP_WRITE_HEADER`; `apps/web/test/backup.web.test.ts`, `apps/web/e2e/backup.spec.ts`; runbook `web.md` "The backup"):
- **The three calls:** `PUT /v1/backup/:lookupId` with `X-Write-Token` and the envelope `{v, kdf, nonce, ct}` (at most 512 KB) answers `{state: "stored"}`; `GET` answers the envelope as it was put; `DELETE` with the token answers `{state: "deleted"}`. Every answer goes to any origin and is never cached; the preflight allows `content-type, x-write-token`.
- **What the server holds:** the envelope's own four fields, as JSON, in the blob store at `backup/<lookupId>`, and a `Backup` row (the lookup ID, `tokenHash(writeToken)`, the size, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`). The envelope passes through the API: 512 KB fits a function's body, so there is no presigned round trip (pairing's files need one only because they reach 40 MB).
- **The write token:** the first put claims a lookup ID for its token, in one statement (`INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE … WHERE` the stored hash is the token's); another token gets 401. The row is written before the blob, so a refused caller never changes the stored envelope. Of two first puts at once, one wins and the other gets 401.
- **Reading needs no token and no database.** The lookup ID is a 256-bit secret from the TV's master key, the envelope is ciphertext without that key, and a restore right after a wipe is what the backup is for: it reads only the blob store.
- **Deleting** checks the token against the row (constant time), then deletes the blob, then the row: a row left without its blob could be claimed anew while the old envelope still read.
- **Retention:** each put first sweeps at most 20 backups last updated 180 days or more ago, the oldest first (each blob, then the rows that are still old). A TV back after that long writes again under its own ID.
- **Rate limits** per caller address through `rateKey`: 30 writes (puts and deletes) and 30 reads in 10 minutes, each counted before anything else is looked at.
- **Refusals:** 404 a malformed lookup ID (before the token) and a get or delete of nothing; 401 a missing, malformed or other token (before the body is read); 400 and 413 for the body; 429 with `Retry-After`; 503 when the database or the store fails, logging only the error's kind.
- **No migration:** step 29a's `Backup` table has every column.
- **Tests:** 27 against Postgres (`pnpm test:web`, 72 in all): each answer; the row and the blob after each call; the largest envelope; the two first puts at once; the sweep's edge at exactly 180 days, its bound, a blob that won't go, a row updated while it runs; the limits, keyed on an HMAC; the route through the process's services, where every row, blob and log line is searched for the profile's markers, the write token and the key. One over HTTP against `next start` (`pnpm e2e:web`, 20 in all): a TV calling with `Origin: null` puts, gets and opens, is refused with another token, deletes.
- **Mutation:** `artifacts/mutate32a.py`, 76 written first: 74 caught, then two test gaps closed (the blob key's prefix was only ever derived; the route's own clock and rate secret): 76/76.

### 3.3 Encryption and key scheme

All primitives come from **tweetnacl** (X25519, XSalsa20-Poly1305, Ed25519) and **@noble/hashes** (SHA-256, HMAC, HKDF, scrypt). Both are pure JavaScript, so they work on Chromium 53 and on `file://`, where WebCrypto `subtle` may not be available. The phone page uses the same `packages/crypto`, and test vectors are shared between TV and web.

**A. Pairing (end-to-end; the server only ever sees ciphertext)**
1. The TV generates an ephemeral X25519 keypair `(tvPk, tvSk)`, held in memory only. It calls `POST /v1/pair {tvPk}`. The server stores `sha256(code)`, `tvPk` and `sha256(pollToken)`. The code is 8 Crockford base32 characters (about 40 bits), expires after 10 minutes, and is rate-limited per IP.
2. The TV shows a QR code for `https://<domain>/p#c=<code>&k=<tvPk>`. The URL fragment is never sent to the server. It also shows the code in large text, so a family member elsewhere can type it at `<domain>/p`.
3. The phone page (English and Arabic, RTL) offers three forms: Xtream (server, user, password), M3U URL, or M3U file. Each takes optional EPG URLs and a name. The page never contacts the provider.
4. The phone runs `ct = nacl.box(JSON(payload), nonce, tvPk, phoneEphSk)` and posts `{phonePk, nonce, ct}`.
   - **File uploads:** a random `fileKey` encrypts the file in 1 MB `secretbox` frames (nonce = base + counter), uploaded through the presigned PUT.
   - **Decryption key:** `fileKey` and the object key travel inside the boxed payload.
   - **On the TV:** it decrypts and parses frame by frame (step 31d2a: the frames open in the pair worker, and the ingest worker parses them from a `MessageChannel` port; copied, not transferred, since Chromium 53 drops a port message with a transferred buffer, step 31d2b2a).
5. The TV decrypts the payload, validates it against the provider directly, and posts only a status category back. The server deletes the payload once the TV reads it.

**B. Backup (restores automatically after the webOS wipe, no user action needed)**
```
deviceSecret = DUID (Tizen) | LGUDID (webOS) | fallback: random 128-bit "Backup code" shown in Settings
master     = scrypt(deviceSecret, salt="lablo.backup.v1|"+platform+"|"+appId, N=2^15, r=8, p=1, dkLen=32)
             (derived once in a worker, a few seconds on 2018 TVs, then cached in IDB)
lookupId   = b64url(HMAC-SHA256(master, "lookup"))
writeToken = HMAC-SHA256(master, "write")          server stores sha256(writeToken) on first write
encKey     = HKDF-SHA256(master, info="enc")       localKey = HKDF-SHA256(master, info="local")
envelope   = {v:1, kdf:"scrypt-15-8-1", nonce:24B, ct: secretbox(gzip(profileJSON), nonce, encKey)}
```
- **Restore.** At startup, if IDB has no profile, derive `master`, GET the envelope, decrypt it, show "Welcome back", and re-ingest catalogs in the background.
- **Upload.** 60 s after a change, at most once every 5 minutes, and whenever the app is hidden.
- **Credentials on the TV** are not encrypted at rest (step 32e, ADR-013, Omar's choice). They stay in the app's IndexedDB: in the profile and in every stored stream URL. Everything that leaves the TV is sealed (the pairing, the backup), and our servers never hold a login in plain text. `localKey` is still derived, and unused.
- **Threat model, honestly stated.**
  - **Protected:** a database leak, insiders, and any request for plaintext (we don't have it).
  - **Not protected:** someone who has both our database and the physical TV's device ID.
  - **Later hardening:** an optional user PIN mixed into the KDF.
- **Settings.** The backup is on by default and disclosed during onboarding, with a toggle in Settings ("include login details" is a separate toggle).

**Built in step 32b: the TV's backup keys** (`packages/crypto/src/backup-code.ts`; `packages/data/src`: `backup-service.ts`, `backup.worker.ts`, `check-backup.ts`, `client.ts`'s `createBackupClient`; `apps/tv/src/backup/backup-request.ts`, `data/engine.ts`'s `prepareBackup`, `data/workers.ts`; `tv-defines.config.ts`'s `appIdFor` and the `__APP_ID__` define; `platforms/webos/appinfo.json`'s `requiredACG`; the vectors page's backup run):
- **The secret.** `backupRequest(platform, consent, build)` reads `Platform.deviceSecret()` (Tizen's DUID, webOS's LGUDID through luna) only when the profile's `consent` is `true`; "Not now" and "not asked yet" never call it. A missing, blank or refused ID counts as none, and the worker then uses the backup code. The ID is read on the main thread (the platforms' APIs exist only there) and goes to the worker in the request; it is never sent or logged.
- **The salt's app id** is a build constant, `__APP_ID__` (`appIdFor`): Tizen `config.xml`'s application id (`LabloIPTV1.Lablo`), webOS `appinfo.json`'s `id` (`com.lablo.iptv`), the desktop app webOS's. A test pins it to the manifests. Both ids are placeholders until step 45 and permanent after: a changed id orphans every backup.
- **The backup worker,** a worker of its own, started for each call and ended after it (the config worker with scrypt added measured 148.7 KB of its 150; on its own the backup worker is 105.5 KB, leaving 32c room for the gzip and the envelope). `prepare({platform, appId, secret})` answers `{source: 'device' | 'code', derived}` and pushes scrypt's progress in whole percents:
  - **the backup code** (`kv` `backup.code`), when there is no device secret: the kept one, or 16 new bytes of `randomBytes`, written in the same transaction that found none (two workers at once keep one code), before anything is derived from it. A code that can't be kept fails the call. `kv` now holds one thing the providers can't rebuild.
  - **the master** (`kv` `backup.master`: `{source, check, master}`): `check` is b64url(SHA-256(UTF-8(salt + "|" + secret))). A kept master whose check matches is used without scrypt; otherwise scrypt runs and the record is replaced (a consent given later derives again; the code stays kept for a later "Not now"). A master that can't be written still answers this call; a broken record is replaced. No key leaves the worker: 32c's upload and restore run in it.
- **The code's text** (`backupCodeText`, `readBackupCode`): the 128-bit number in base 32, big-endian, as 26 Crockford characters, the first 0 to 7. Typed back, spaces and dashes are dropped, lower case read as upper, I and L as 1, O as 0. scrypt takes those 26 characters.
- **The engine:** `DataEngine.prepareBackup(request, onProgress)`, beside the refresh slot. The app doesn't call it yet: 32c does (the upload's first run, the restore at a start with no profile).
- **webOS:** the app's `requiredACG` is `["deviceinfo.query"]` (the LGUDID's group), which `acg.test.ts`'s scan of the app's source now requires.
- **On the old engines:** the vectors page runs `@lablo/data/check-backup` in a second Blob worker against the engine's own IndexedDB (a database of its own, deleted first and last): the vectors' device secret gives their master and lookup ID, a second call reads the master back without scrypt, a call with no secret makes a code, keeps it and reads it back.
- **Found while building, for 32e:** with the master cached in IndexedDB (as this section's scheme says), `localKey` protects nothing against a copied IndexedDB either, whatever the secret: the master is beside the logins. 32e has to keep its promise by other means (not caching what derives `localKey`, or saying so plainly). **Decided in 32e (ADR-013):** said plainly; there is no seal (below, "Decided in step 32e").
- **For 32c:** a TV whose source changes (a consent given later, or an ID unreadable at one start) gets a new lookup ID; the copy under the old one should be deleted with the old write token, or it waits for the API's 180-day sweep. (Done in 32c1: the old copies.)
- **Tests:** unit 6,380 (+93); `test/backup-worker-bundle.test.ts` (the bundled worker, 105.5 KB of 150, in the Chromium 53 stand-in realm: the vectors' master, read back, a code made and read back); `e2e/crypto-vectors.spec.ts`; mutation `artifacts/mutate32b.py`, 94 written first: 89 caught, three test gaps closed, two equivalent: 92/92.
- **Merged:** PR #129 as merge commit `57bee9c` on green CI on the first run (verify 16 m 28 s: `pnpm e2e` 150 passed; web 10 m 35 s; media 11 m 41 s; engines 23 m 25 s: all 126 runs as expected, the backup worker 8/8 on Chromium 53, 56 and 68, its scrypt with IndexedDB 1.2 s on 53, 3.4 s on 56, 0.2 s on 68).

**Built in step 32c1: the envelope, the upload and the restore in the backup worker** (`packages/data/src`: `backup-plain.ts`, `backup-api.ts`, `backup-stand-in.ts`, `backup-service.ts`'s `upload` and `restore`, `profile.ts`'s `storedProfileDoc` and `putProfileIfNone`, `check-backup.ts`'s three checks; `packages/protocol/src/backup-limits.ts`; `apps/tv/src/data/engine.ts`'s `uploadBackup` and `restoreBackup`; the harness's `BACKUP_CHECKS` and log line):
- **The plaintext** is the stored profile document's JSON, whole (sources with their logins, guides, favourites, places, settings), UTF-8 and gzipped (fflate, `mtime` 0). A phone-sent file source goes by name (it has no bytes: the storage table above). "Include login details" off comes with its toggle in 32d (a source without its login is a profile version of its own). Read back: a gzip whose trailer says more than 8 MB is refused before inflating; then strict UTF-8, JSON, a whole version of 1 or more.
- **The size** is checked before sealing: the gzip plus secretbox's 16-byte tag must fit `BACKUP_CT_MAX_BYTES` (392,448 bytes, so the body stays within 512 KB); else `too-large`, nothing sent.
- **`upload({platform, appId, secret, base})`:** `none` with no profile, a newer one, or no source yet. The master as `prepare` keeps it. `same` when the document's hash (b64url(SHA-256(its JSON))) and the lookup ID are the last send's (`kv` `backup.sent`: `{lookupId, token, hash, old}`). The first send under a lookup ID asks `GET` first: **a backup this TV hasn't seen is never written over** (`held`; after a wipe whose look-up failed, the new profile would otherwise replace the old backup; 32d offers to restore or replace it). The lookup ID is then claimed in `backup.sent` (hash `''`) before the `PUT`, so a failed `PUT` is sent again without the `GET`. After a send under a new lookup ID (a consent given later), the copy under the one before is deleted with its own token; 200, 404 and 401 forget it, anything else keeps it in `old` for the next upload.
- **`restore({…, base})`** (the device ID; 32d's typed code has its own path): `kept` when a profile is there (checked first and again inside the write). The master is derived in memory, and **nothing is written until a backup is restored**, so a look-up that finds nothing (`none`), finds something else (`unreadable`), a newer app's profile (`newer`), or fails (`failed`), or is ended by the app's time limit, keeps nothing derived. Restored (older versions upgraded): the profile, the master record and `backup.sent` (the hash of the document as the server holds it, so an upgraded one is sent again) in one transaction; the answer gives the profile's language and its number of sources.
- **Requests** (`BackupApi`): `fetchTransport` (`PUT` and `DELETE` added), 20 s for each whole answer (Chromium 53 can't abort a fetch: a late one is cancelled), only a 200 `GET`'s body read, capped at 512 KB plus 1 KB; the `PUT`'s body written by hand. Results never hold a key, a token or a URL.
- **The engine:** `uploadBackup(request)` and `restoreBackup(request, {onProgress, cancel})`, each in a backup worker of its own beside the refresh slot; a cancel (32c2's time limit) rejects with `CancelError` and ends the worker.
- **`@lablo/protocol/backup-limits`:** the limits and the header in a module that imports nothing, so the backup worker reads them without zod (a test pins that).
- **The worker's size:** 131.4 KB of its 150 (105.5 KB in 32b: fflate's deflate, core-js's `JSON.stringify` fix, the profile's migrations and the client); main.js's own code 459.5 KB (Tizen) and 446.8 KB (webOS) of 500.
- **On the old engines:** `checkBackup` sends a profile to the API in memory (`backupStandIn`, the 32a server's rules: the claim by token hash, 401, 404, 413, 400), sends nothing for it unchanged, then wipes its database and restores it, on Chromium 53, 56 and 68 (runbook old-engines.md item 9). First run here: 11/11, the restore 0.8 s on 53 and 0.1 s on 68.
- **Tests:** unit 6,534 (+154): `backup-plain.test.ts` 25, `backup-api.test.ts` 23, `backup-stand-in.test.ts` 17, `backup-service-sync.test.ts` 83 (each upload and restore state; the ciphertext's exact edge, found with a SHA-256 stream; the old copies; a profile written while the look-up ran; the write all or nothing; the worker over RPC), `profile.test.ts` +2, the engine's +2, the harness's and the protocol's; `test/backup-worker-bundle.test.ts` +1 (the bundled worker sends and restores in the Chromium 53 stand-in realm through a stand-in `fetch`). Mutation `artifacts/mutate32c1.py`, 122 written first: 119 caught, 2 test gaps closed, 1 equivalent: 121/121.
- **Merged:** PR #130 as merge commit `9c9c04e` on green CI (verify 13 m 25 s: `pnpm e2e` 150 passed; web 2 m 26 s; engines 24 m 49 s: all 126 runs as expected, the backup worker 11/11 on Chromium 53, 56 and 68, the restore after a wipe 1.4 s on 53, 4.2 s on 56, 0.2 s on 68; media green on a rerun of the `epg-declared-300mb` timing flake).

**Built in step 32c2: the look-up, "Welcome back" and the upload schedule in the app** (`apps/tv/src/backup/`: `lookup-request.ts`, `backup-start.ts`, `BackupStartApp.tsx`, `backup-schedule.ts`, `backup-request.ts`'s `readDeviceSecret` and `consentOf`; `main.tsx`'s `startWithoutProfile` and `startWith`; `data/engine.ts`'s `onProfileChanged`; `data/refresh-scheduler.ts`'s `start(firstMs)`; `test-hook.ts`; the sources screen's file line; the catalogs; `packages/platform-browser`'s `?device=`; `tools/engines/src/backup-stand-in.ts`; `e2e/backup.spec.ts`):
- **The look-up** (Omar's decision, RELAY step 32): a start whose profile read gives none calls `startWithoutProfile`, which reads the device ID with no consent asked (`lookupRequest`, the only such read: a source scan pins its one caller and that `deviceSecret` is called only by `readDeviceSecret`, which `backupRequest` calls only after "Allow"). No ID (the browser, a refused read): onboarding at once. Else **the start waits** behind "Looking for your backup" (scrypt's progress bar, "Skip"; Back skips too), because onboarding's first answer creates a profile and a restore never writes over one. `restoreBackup` runs with a cancel: Skip, Back, or **30 s** (`LOOKUP_LIMIT_MS`; the whole restore took 4.2 s on CI's Chromium 56). **However it ends, the profile is read again**: one with a source is a restore (a cancel can come just after the restore committed), anything else is onboarding. An answer that says something went wrong (`unreadable`, `newer`, `failed`) is logged; the time limit losing a real backup costs nothing, since the first upload finds it (`held`) and 32d offers to restore it.
- **"Welcome back"** in the restored profile's language (else the TV's), with "Continue", for 5 s (`WELCOME_MS`) or until OK, so a restore needs no key; when the profile has a file sent from a phone, one line says to send it again (Settings, Sources, Add from your phone). Then the live screen: **the channel watched last doesn't play by itself** after a restore (the catalogs are being read again, and a months-old stream address may be stale; the next start resumes as usual), and **the refresh schedule's first check comes at once** (`start(0)`), so the catalogs come back through `DataEngine.refresh` and the blocklist as after webOS's wipe on an update. The sources screen says a file source with no catalog is "Not loaded yet: send this file again from your phone."
- **The upload schedule** (`createBackupSchedule`, from the live screen on): the data engine calls `onProfileChanged` after an edit that changed the profile and after a catalog refresh that committed; a run comes 60 s after the first change not yet sent, never sooner than 5 minutes after the last run began (a movie's place, kept every 10 s, is sent at most every 5 minutes), and at once when the app is hidden with a change pending (two runs at most for each hide, so the player's hide-time save goes too; none after a failure). Its own start counts as a change (onboarding wrote before the live screen opened; the worker answers `same` when nothing changed). Each run reads the profile's consent: the device ID only after "Allow" (`backupRequest`), else the backup code. `held` ends the runs for the session (32d shows it); `failed` and a worker that died keep the change for the next run, 5 minutes on; `held`, `failed`, `too-large` and a dead worker are logged. The config's `disable-app` stops it with the refreshes.
- **The consent text** (en, ar) says the ID is used "to find and encrypt" the backup.
- **The test hook:** `backupIdle()` (no look-up or upload running) and `uploadBackup()` (a run now, its state). The old-engine walk's idle wait (`CONFIG_IDLE`) waits for both workers; the seeded variant keeps `backupIdle`. The harness's TV builds give no device ID (no `webapis`, no `PalmServiceBridge`), so the walks never look up; the desktop dev app takes `?device=` from a test.
- **The stand-in API:** `BackupStandIn` (`tools/engines`), the backup routes over HTTP with the 32a rules (the claim by token hash, 401, 404, 413, 400, the preflight for `content-type, x-write-token`), with a hold on `GET`s; answered by the old-engine walk's pair server and the e2e's config server.
- **Sizes:** main.js's own code 469.1 KB (Tizen) and 456.4 KB (webOS) of 500 (+9.6 KB: the two screens, the schedule, the look-up's state and the catalogs' texts); the workers unchanged (the backup worker 131.2 KB).
- **Tests:** unit 6,621 (+87): `backup-schedule.test.ts` 19, `backup-start.test.ts` 16, `lookup-request.test.ts` 6, the stand-in's 9, the engine's +1, the refresh schedule's +1, the test hook's +1, the harness's +3, the sources screen's and the browser platform's; `e2e/backup.spec.ts` 4 (a fresh install finds nothing, an upload `PUT`, a wipe, "Looking…" with its `GET` held, "Welcome back", the live list filled with no key, in en and ar; Skip; no device ID); `pnpm e2e` 154 locally; `verify:engines --engine chrome53 --engine chrome68` 22/22. Mutation `artifacts/mutate32c2.py`, 144 written first: 13 pieces of code that could never decide anything taken out, 6 test gaps closed, 1 equivalent: 135/135.
- **Merged:** PR #131 merged as merge commit `e06d803` on green CI on the first run (web 2 m 5 s; verify 14 m: unit 6,620 with one skipped, `pnpm e2e` 154 passed; engines 22 m 28 s: all 126 runs as expected on Chromium 53, 56 and 68, 89 seeded failures caught, the backup worker 11/11 on each, its restore 1.0 s on 53, 3.2 s on 56, 0.1 s on 68; media 24 m 36 s).

**Built in step 32d1: the proof, a simulated webOS wipe restores automatically** (step 32's own "Done when"; `e2e/backup-api.spec.ts`; `tools/engines/src/backup-walk.ts`, `variants.ts`'s `backup` screen and `seed-backup-luna-refused`, `verify-engines.ts`; `test/engines-backup.test.ts`). No TV or API code changed: the restore built in 32c2 held as it was.
- **Against the real API** (`e2e:web`'s `tv` project, through the test front): the TV's dev app with a test device ID, `next start` with `lablo_e2e`, the mock provider. A first start's look-up gets 404 from the real route; onboarding adds the source (English: an Xtream login; Arabic: an M3U link, its guide link and an Arabic name); the English test writes two favourites, a movie's place and a show's episode into the stored profile (no screen adds a favourite before step 35). The upload (`GET 404`, `PUT 200`, a real preflight before it) is then opened in Node with keys derived from the same device ID: **the envelope is exactly the stored document**, its ciphertext is no gzip stream, and another device's key doesn't open it. **Nothing in the clear:** the server, the login, the links and the names (the source's, the favourites') are in the opened envelope and on the TV, and in none of what the web app received, its rows or its blob; the `Backup` row holds the token's hash and the blob's size. Then the wipe and a start with no key: "Welcome back" with the document back exactly as the envelope held it, then the live screen with the channels and their guide.
- **On the old engines** (a new screen, `backup`, webOS only): the harness copies each variant's webOS build to `webos-sim` with a stand-in luna bridge before its main.js (ES5): the LGUDID's call (`luna://com.webos.service.sm/deviceid/getIDs`) answers a fixed ID, every other call is refused, each URI asked is recorded. The bundle runs unchanged, through its own `readLgudid`. Each walk (English, then Arabic): a fresh database and no backup on the stand-in; the look-up finds nothing; onboarding by phone; "Start watching"; an upload (`GET 404`, `PUT 200`); the database deleted from the blank page; the same address again with no key: "Welcome back" (in the walk's language, one source, inside the title-safe area, no label cut), then the live screen with its rows filled. The stand-in must have answered exactly `GET 404, GET 404, PUT 200, GET 200`, all under the lookup ID the simulated LGUDID gives (derived by the harness itself); the profile read back holds the phone's source, the language and `consent: true`.
- **Its seed:** `seed-backup-luna-refused` refuses every luna call (an app without its ACG group): the TV reads no ID, its backup goes under a backup code, and after the wipe the start has nothing to look for, so the walk must fail ("onboarding, not Welcome back").
- **Found by the first old-engine run:** on Chromium 53 the walk's probe took the boot's placeholder (a not-ready onboarding root, drawn while the profile is read) for onboarding and stopped before "Welcome back"; 68 was quick enough to pass. The probe counts only a ready onboarding now, as the walk's own probe does.
- **Found by CI's first run (PR #132):** on CI's Chromium 53 the Arabic walk read "Welcome back" in English: the start screen drew it with the TV's locale first and switched to the restored language only in an effect after an async `platform.info()`, so a slow engine painted the English frame. `BackupStartApp` now reads the TV's locale once and works out the start's locale while drawing (`startLocale`), setting `<html lang dir>` and the remote's direction in a layout effect, before the paint. The same run found `backup-api.spec.ts` in the plain `pnpm e2e` (no API there: `GET 0`); it is in the chromium project's ignore list now, with `pairing-api` and `no-plaintext`.
- **Merged:** PR #132 merged as merge commit `7c54639` on green CI (the second run: web 7 m; verify 16.6 m after one rerun of the `auto-next` ar-SA flake, `pnpm e2e` 154 passed; engines 26 m: all 132 runs as expected on Chromium 53, 56 and 68, 92 seeded failures caught, the backup walk 1.5 s / 9.1 s on 53, 3.6 s / 11.3 s on 56, 0.5 s / 6.5 s on 68; media 24 m). Mutation `artifacts/mutate32d1.py`: 95/95 (5 test gaps closed).

**Built in step 32d2a: the backup's status, off, and a held backup, in the worker** (`packages/data/src`: `backup-service.ts`'s `status`, `setEnabled`, `restoreHeld`, `replaceHeld`, `upload`'s `off`, `SentRecord.sentAt`; `profile.ts`'s `putProfileOver`; `check-backup.ts`'s five checks; `apps/tv/src/data/engine.ts`'s `backupStatus`, `setBackup`, `restoreHeldBackup`, `replaceHeldBackup`; the harness's `BACKUP_CHECKS`). Settings' page (32d2b) is the only caller; nothing in the app turns the backup off yet.
- **On or off** is `kv`'s `backup.off` (`true`; no key is on, as onboarding says), not the profile: any backup that exists was made while on, so the profile would only ever bring back "on", and a profile version would be spent. A wipe forgets it, and the onboarding after a wipe discloses the backup again. ("Include login details", 32d4, goes in the profile instead: a backup made without logins must bring that choice back.)
- **Off** (`setEnabled({…, on: false})`): the flag is written first, so no upload sends after it; then every copy this TV wrote (the lookup ID it writes and the old ones) and the copy under this request's lookup ID (a held one too: this TV's secret wrote it, and off means no copy) are deleted with their own tokens. What can't be deleted yet stays in `backup.sent` under no lookup ID of its own (`lookupId: ''`), and each later upload tries it again and answers `off`, with no `GET` or `PUT` and no key derived. **On** removes the flag; the next upload sends as a first send does, and a copy of its own lookup ID whose delete never went through is this TV's own, written over without the `GET` (else it would read as `held`).
- **`status`**: on or off, the master's source (the device ID after "Allow", else the code), the backup code only while on with the code as the secret (made and kept then, as `prepare` does), when the copy on the server was last written (`backup.sent.sentAt`: a send, a restore; kept over a claim and over an unchanged upload; a record from before has none), and whether copies are still to delete. It derives nothing.
- **A held backup:** `restoreHeld` reads and opens it as the look-up does (the shared `fetch`), then writes it over the profile in one transaction with its send record, the replaced profile kept as a snapshot (`restore`; `putProfileOver` never writes over a newer app's profile: `kept`); copies this TV wrote under another lookup ID stay to delete. `replaceHeld` is an upload without the `GET` (the same master gives the same write token, so the API takes the `PUT`).
- **Verified (leg 107):** unit 6,719 (+49: `backup-service-sync.test.ts` +43, `profile.test.ts` +1, `check-backup.test.ts` +1, `tv-data-engine.test.ts` +1, the harness's); lint, typecheck; `build:tv`: the backup worker 134.3 KB of 150 (+3.1), main.js 469.5 KB (Tizen) and 456.8 KB (webOS); `verify:engines --engine chrome53 --engine chrome68` 24/24, the backup worker 16/16 (4.7 s on 53, 0.8 s on 68); mutation `artifacts/mutate32d2a.py`, 67 written first: 2 pieces of dead code out before the run (`forget`'s record flag, a type check in `putProfileOver`), 5 test gaps closed, 67/67.
- **The old engines:** the backup worker's checks grow from 11 to 16 (`backup.held`, `backup.restoreHeld`, `backup.replaceHeld`, `backup.off`, `backup.on`) against the in-memory stand-in, through each engine's own IndexedDB.
- **Not yet:** two backup workers at once (the page's switch while the schedule's upload runs) could let a `PUT` land after off's `DELETE`; the next upload, answering `off`, deletes it again. 32d2b keeps the two apart (the page waits for the schedule's run, and stops it while it switches). **Done in 32d2b** (the schedule's `hold`, below).
- **Merged:** PR #133 merged as merge commit `c8f8c53` on green CI on the first run (web 5 m; verify 13.6 m, `pnpm e2e` 154 passed; engines 25 m: all 132 runs as expected on Chromium 53, 56 and 68, the backup worker 16/16 on each, 5.2 s on 53, 16.1 s on 56, 0.8 s on 68; media 25 m).

**Built in step 32d2b: Settings' backup page** (`apps/tv/src/settings`: `backup-state.ts`, `BackupPage.tsx`, `settings-state.ts`'s third row, `SettingsScreen.tsx`, `settings.css`; `apps/tv/src/backup`: `backup-schedule.ts`'s `hold`, `replaceNow` and `held`, `backup-start.ts`'s `restored`; `apps/tv/src/data/params.ts`'s `readRestored` and `restartUrl`; `main.tsx`'s `backupCall`, `quiet`, the restart and `startRestored`; `packages/data/src/backup-service.ts`'s `RESTORE_CLEARS`; `check-backup.ts`'s `backup.restoreHeld`; the catalogs; `e2e/backup-settings.spec.ts`):
- **The page** (Settings' third row, "Backup" / «النسخ الاحتياطي», after "Sources" so the earlier rows' paths stay): the header and one line on what the backup is; the switch row ("Backup", "On"/"Off" at its end) and the line under it: what runs ("Turning off…", "Turning on and sending…"), else the session's last run when it didn't send (failed: "The last try to send it failed…", too large), else when the copy on the server was last written ("Last backed up {day}, {time}", "Not backed up yet"); off: "Nothing is sent while it is off", or that the copy on the server is still to delete. While on with the backup code as the secret, the code's box: the 26 characters in groups of four (`dir="ltr"`, never translated, wide letters for a photo) and what it is for. While on with a held backup, the held box: what it is, "Restore it", "Replace it" (dimmed while an action runs, with its line). A notice in the warning colour when an action didn't work (off, on, the replace, the restore: the server, a newer app's backup, anything else: "Nothing on this TV was changed"). Every text from the catalogs (en, ar).
- **Asking first:** off ("Turn off backup? The copy on the server is deleted now."), "Restore it" (the profile on the TV is replaced and Lablo starts again) and "Replace it" (what the old backup holds can't be brought back) open a confirm dialog with "Cancel" focused first. **Refined while building:** "Replace it" asks too, though the shape had only the restore asking: it is the one action that loses data for good (the restore keeps the replaced profile as a snapshot). Turning on asks nothing.
- **Reads:** the worker's status when the page opens and after each action, with the schedule's request (the device ID only after "Allow"), without the API base. An older read's answer or failure is dropped; a failed read shows "Couldn't read the backup's settings" and nothing older.
- **One backup worker at a time** (32d2a's "Not yet"): the schedule's `hold(job)` waits for the run in progress and starts none until `job` settles (a run asked meanwhile comes after; a dispose meanwhile starts nothing); off and on run inside it, then on asks a run at once (`uploadNow`). "Replace it" is a run of the schedule's own (`replaceNow`: `replaceHeldBackup` in place of `uploadBackup`), so it is one at a time by construction. `held` follows the answers: `held` sets it, any other answer but `failed` or a dead worker clears it (the timed runs come back). The test hook's `backupIdle` counts a hold.
- **"Restore it"** runs inside the hold and inside `quiet` (main.tsx: the refresh schedule disposed, which cancels its refresh, then the data engine's slot waited for, so no catalog is written across the clear). **The restore clears every catalog and guide** with the profile it replaces, in its transaction (`RESTORE_CLEARS`: `sources_meta`, the catalog stores, the EPG stores; the config and the backup records in `kv` stay). **Found while building:** source ids start again at `s1` after a wipe (`nextSourceId`), so the held backup's `s1` can be another server than the replaced profile's; its catalog, fresh, would have shown under the restored source and not been read again for hours (`catalogRefreshDue`). The e2e proves it: the replaced profile's playlist ("Second Channel") is gone and the restored one's ("Family News") is read.
- **The app starts again by a reload:** the player closes, the refreshes and uploads stop (the gate's stop), then `location.replace` with `restored=1` first in the query (`restartUrl`; before a `source`, which runs to the end; once; no fragment). The start reads the profile and, with the mark (`readRestored`), shows "Welcome back" at once on it (`createBackupStart` with `restored`: no look-up, no profile read again), then the live screen as after a restore at a first start: `start(0)`, the channel watched last not played. Why a reload: the live screen, the player and their lifecycle subscriptions have no dispose path, and the start is the one path already proved to build them all; the URL works on every engine with no storage. A later reload with the mark would show "Welcome back" again, harmlessly. A restore that didn't restore starts the refreshes again and says why.
- **Sizes:** main.js's own code 489.3 KB (Tizen) and 476.6 KB (webOS) of 500 (+19.8 KB: about 14 KB is the catalogs' new texts, the Arabic written as `\u06XX` escapes, 6 bytes a letter; about 6 KB the page and the schedule's part). **Only 10.7 KB is left on Tizen:** 32d3's typing screen needs room (see RELAY 32d3). The backup worker 134.4 KB of 150 (+0.1).
- **Tests:** unit 6,933 (+214, most of them the catalogs' per-key checks): `backup-page.test.ts` 39 (the page's reads, actions and focus over a fake engine and schedule; the page and the dialog drawn), `backup-schedule.test.ts` +12, `backup-start.test.ts` +3, `backup-service-sync.test.ts` +1 (and the catalogs untouched in every restore that writes nothing), `params.test.ts` +2, `guides-state.test.ts` +1 (the third row), the pending sum's; `e2e/backup-settings.spec.ts` 3 in plain `pnpm e2e` (157 locally): English with "Not now" (the code shown in groups equals `kv`'s `backup.code`; off: asked, the stand-in's `DELETE 200` and its copy gone, then `uploadBackup()` answers `off` with no request; on: `GET 404`, `PUT 200` at once), Arabic with a held backup ("Replace it": asked, then `PUT 200` with no `GET`, then `same`), and "Restore it" (an Arabic backup over an English profile: the reload, "مرحبًا بعودتك" right to left, `GET 200`, the old playlist's channels and not the replaced one's, the profile back and the replaced one in a `restore` snapshot, then `same`); screenshots `backup-settings-*.png` in en and ar looked at. `verify:engines --engine chrome53 --engine chrome68`: all 24 runs as expected, the backup worker 16/16 (its `backup.restoreHeld` now checks the catalogs cleared in each engine's own IndexedDB; 5.3 s on 53, 1.0 s on 68). Mutation `artifacts/mutate32d2b.py`, 121 written first (one equivalent rewritten before the run): three checks that never decided anything taken out (the schedule's `runHidden` and a hold's release looked at other holds, `run` and `arm` refuse anyway; the page's action looked at `busy`, its OK does), nine test gaps closed, 120/120 (main.tsx's wiring by the e2e spec).
- **The privacy text:** none is written yet (counsel drafts it: the launch plan); onboarding's note already says the backup can be turned off in Settings, which the page now does.
- **Merged:** PR #134 as merge commit `cacdb6b` on green CI on the first run (web 2 m 31 s; verify 18 m 41 s, with `pnpm e2e` and `e2e/backup-settings.spec.ts`; engines 29 m 0 s; media 27 m 29 s).

**Built in step 32d3b: the typed backup code's restore, in the worker** (`packages/data/src`: `backup-service.ts`'s `restoreCode` and its handler, `BackupCodeRequest`; `profile.ts`'s `codeRestoreMayWrite` and `putProfileFromCode`; `check-backup.ts`'s `backup.codeUpload` and `backup.restoreCode`; `apps/tv/src/data/engine.ts`'s `restoreBackupCode`; the harness's `BACKUP_CHECKS` and summary):
- **The request** is the call's platform, app id and origin, and the code as it was typed: the worker reads it with `readBackupCode` (spaces, dashes, lower case, I and L as 1, O as 0) and throws a `TypeError` for text that can't be a code, before IndexedDB opens. The screen (32d3c) checks it first for an instant answer.
- **Where it writes:** no profile, or one of this or an older app version with no source yet: onboarding's answers, saved before the source is added (`codeRestoreMayWrite`). A profile with a source, one that can't be read and one from a newer app answer `kept` before anything is derived. A restore over a TV in use is Settings' "Restore it" (32d2b).
- **The look-up:** the code's master derived in memory (scrypt, `progress`), the backup fetched and opened as the device ID's look-up does (`none`, `unreadable`, `newer`, `failed` keep nothing: a mistyped code leaves nothing derived).
- **The write**, one transaction over the profile, `kv` and `RESTORE_CLEARS`, the profile read again inside it (a source added meanwhile: `kept`): the restored document over the sourceless one with no snapshot (its answers come back with the backup); the code as this TV's own (`backup.code`: Settings shows it); its master (`source: 'code'`); `backup.sent` (the next upload answers `same`); every catalog and guide cleared (onboarding's loader commits a catalog before the profile gets its source, so a load cancelled in its guide step leaves one under `s1`, which the backup's own `s1` would show).
- **The answer** is `BackupRestoreResult` as for the other restores, so the screen reuses 32d2b's restart (`restored=1`: "Welcome back", then the live screen).
- **The old engines:** the backup worker's checks grow from 16 to 18: `backup.codeUpload` (a profile sent under a new backup code) and `backup.restoreCode` (the database wiped, onboarding's answers saved, the code typed in lower case and in two groups: the profile back, the code kept, the next upload `same`). The harness's summary adds the code's restore time ("the code's 1.4 s").
