**Built in step 32d3c: "Restore with a backup code" at the first start** (`apps/tv/src/onboarding/`: `restore-code.ts`, `RestoreCodePage.tsx`, `onboarding-state.ts`'s `sourceRows` and `restoreCode` host, `OnboardingApp.tsx`'s rows, `onboarding.css`'s `onboarding__notice`; `main.tsx`'s host; `@lablo/crypto/backup-code` as an entry point; the catalogs; `e2e/backup-code.spec.ts`):
- **Where:** the source screen's last row, "Use a backup code" («استخدم رمزًا احتياطيًا»), after Xtream, M3U and "Use your phone" (`sourceRows`), with its hint ("If Lablo backed up your sources before, type the code it showed"). Why there: it is the screen that asks where the channels come from, and a restore is the other answer; it comes after the consent, which the restored profile replaces with its own; and the device ID's look-up has already run before onboarding, so whoever has a backup at this screen has one under a code. The row shows whenever main.tsx gives onboarding the host (every build has an API origin: the dev one when the build names none).
- **The screen** (`restore-code`, on onboarding's navigator): the header ("Restore with a backup code"), one line on where the code is (Backup in Settings, its 26 letters and digits), one field (`dir="ltr"`; the text drawn in upper case and in groups of four as typed: spaces and dashes dropped, the caret at the end), "Restore". "Restore" checks the field first (`codeProblem`): nothing typed (`required`), a wrong count of letters and digits (`length`), or a character no code has (`invalid`): the problem under the field, the focus back on it, no worker. `@lablo/crypto/backup-code` gives `readBackupCode` without the rest of crypto (and its polyfills).
- **Restoring:** "Opening your backup…", scrypt's progress and "Cancel" (Back too: the worker cancelled, the field again at once). It first waits until the data engine's refresh slot is free (main's `refreshSlotFree`, as Settings' "Restore it"): a load cancelled just before may still be committing a catalog, which the restore's clear must come after. The time limit is the look-up's 30 s (`CODE_LIMIT_MS`, pinned equal to `LOOKUP_LIMIT_MS` by its test: importing it would close an import cycle through onboarding's start), from the worker's start.
- **The answers:** `restored` → the app starts again as after Settings' "Restore it" (`restartUrl`, `restored=1`: "Welcome back" in the backup's language, then the live screen with the catalogs read at once); `kept` (a source got there meanwhile) → a plain reload onto that profile. `none` → "No backup was found for this code. Check it and try again." with the focus on the field; the others with the focus on "Restore": `newer`, `unreadable` and a worker that died in Settings' restore texts (update first; "Nothing on this TV was changed"), `network`/`timeout` and the time limit as the connection, `rate` ("Too many tries. Wait a few minutes"), `status` (the service had a problem). Each leaves onboarding where it was: the worker wrote nothing.
- **What is trusted:** the worker's answers say what it wrote. A rejection (Cancel, the time limit, a worker that died) may come just after the restore committed, so the profile is read again then, and a source there is a restore. A cancelled restore's own `restored` or `kept` still starts the app; its other answers are dropped; the app starts again once.
- **Text:** 14 catalog keys (en, ar): the row and its hint, the screen's title and line, the field, its hint and three problems, the restoring title and line, and three answers; Settings' and Backup's names as isolated arguments, the code never translated.
- **Sizes:** main.js 453.8 → 462.0 KB own code on Tizen (38 KB left; the ~3 KB guess counted the catalogs, not the screen's own code), 441.1 → 449.3 KB on webOS; the workers unchanged.

The mistyped code is the walk's own proof that it can fail (a wrong code is what a person gets wrong), so it has no seed.

**Built in step 32d4a: the backup without logins, on the data side** (`packages/data/src`: `logins.ts`, `profile.ts`'s version 9, `SourceWithoutLogin`, `StoredSource`, `backupLogins`, the `backupLogins` edit and `GuideChange` `link`; `epg-sources.ts`'s `GuideWithoutLink` and `StoredGuide`; `backup-service.ts`'s send; `account-check.ts`; `check-backup.ts`'s two checks; `apps/tv/src`: `data/engine.ts`'s `RefreshJob` and `NeedsLoginError`, `onboarding/engine-loader.ts`'s `ingestSourceOf` and `catalogOf`, `data/refresh-scheduler.ts`, `live/live-state.ts`'s short EPG, `settings/sources-state.ts` and `guides-state.ts`, `player/player-memory.ts`'s `lastChannelOf`; the harness's `BACKUP_CHECKS`). Nothing in the app turns the logins off yet: 32d4b's Settings row is the only caller of the edit.
- **What a login is** (RELAY 32d4's shape): an Xtream source's user name and password, an M3U source's link whole, each of the user's guide links whole, and, found while building, **each channel played last's stream URL and catch-up template** (`player.last` and `channels` keep the channel's catalog item, whose URL is `/live/<user>/<password>/…`). Kept: every id, name, order, favourite, place, offset and mapping, an Xtream source's server (`base`), a link's origin (`scheme://host[:port]`, lower case; `''` when its authority is more than a host and a port, or it isn't http(s)), and the channels played last with `url: ''` and `catchup: null`.
- **Profile version 9:** a source may be `{…, account: null, base}` (Xtream) or `{…, url: null, origin}` (M3U), a guide `{id, url: null, origin, offsetMinutes?}`; `backupLogins: false` keeps the choice (no field: the logins go). The migration from 8 changes only the version, so an older app refuses the document (`newer`) instead of building a URL from a missing login. 32e takes version 10.
- **The envelope:** `upload` sends `backupDoc(doc)`: `withoutLogins` when the profile says so, before gzip and sealing, from the stored document as it is (nothing written back). `same` compares the stripped text, so turning the choice either way sends at the next upload, and a login changed while off sends nothing. The strip copies field by field and keeps each field's place: stripped twice, or stripped after a login is given back, the JSON is the same.
- **A restore** brings the document back as it was sent: the sources and guides without their logins and `backupLogins: false`, so logins given again stay on the TV.
- **Never refreshed without a login:** `DataEngine.refresh` takes a `RefreshJob` whose source may lack its login (`ingestSourceOf` gives what the source kept) and refuses it before anything (`NeedsLoginError`, code `needs-login`: no refresh slot, no worker, no blocklist read); the ingest worker's job still always carries a login. `epgGuides` lists no guide without its link and no panel guide without its login, so `refreshEpg` never reads one. The refresh schedule takes `needs-login` as quietly as the blocklist's refusal (held 2 h as a failure, nothing in the console). No short EPG is asked through such a source, `sourceAccount` gives none, and **the start doesn't play a last channel with no URL**; Home's "Recent channels" still lists it and plays it from the catalog by key.
- **For 32d4b:** the profile edits `{kind: 'backupLogins', on}` and `GuideChange` `{op: 'link', id, url}` (a guide's link given back in its place, with its id and zone; a link listed already changes nothing); a source's login goes back through the `source` edit. Until then the sources and guides screens list such a row with the server it kept.
- **The old engines:** the backup worker's checks grow from 18 to 20: `backup.noLogins` (an upload with the choice off: only a `PUT`; the envelope, opened with the kept master's key, is exactly the stripped document, holds none of the profile's logins, links or stream path, and keeps the servers' origins and the choice) and `backup.restoreNoLogins` ("Restore it" over another profile, no scrypt: the stripped document back with the choice, then `same`).
- **Sizes:** main.js's own code 462.7 KB (Tizen, +0.6) and 450.0 KB (webOS) of 500; the backup worker 136.9 KB of 150 (+1.4).

- **Settings › Backup's second row,** "Include logins and links", shows only while the backup is on with the profile read (`switchRows`: 2 rows, else 1; a focus on it clamps to the switch when it goes). OK switches it at once both ways and asks nothing: the `backupLogins` edit inside the upload schedule's `hold`, then `uploadNow` (off takes them out of the copy on the server now; it loses nothing, the TV keeps them). Its line: on, that they are in the backup, encrypted; off, that they stay on this TV and are typed again after a restore; "Sending…" while it runs; a failure is the page's notice (`logins-failed`). The page reads the profile with the status at each opening (`includesLogins`: in unless the document says `backupLogins: false`).

Its body says the channels are loading only when a source will load by itself (`sources > files + logins`); otherwise just that the sources, favourites and settings are back.
- **The sources rows:** a source without its login says "Needs its login" (Xtream) or "Needs its link" (M3U) in the warning colour, after the server or origin it kept. OK opens the login form on such a row, and on a row whose last save failed; on any other row OK does nothing, as before. The page draws its own screen (`list.screen`), never `nav.top`, which is the form's while it shows.

The checks are onboarding's `checkForm` for those fields only; a refused field takes the focus. "Save" closes it and writes the source under its own id, name and `added`.
- **The save:** the `source` edit first (what was typed is kept even if the server refuses it or doesn't answer), the rows read again (the mark goes), then that source's own refresh: `DataEngine.refresh` inside main's `quiet` (the schedule stopped and started again), not `refreshNow`, which reads only the guides and would hold the earlier `needs-login` refusal for 2 h. A refresh that committed asks `refreshNow` for its guides and tells the screens (`sourceAdded`, shared with the phone's add). The row says "Updating…", then when it was updated, or why it failed (`errorText`: a refused login says so), and OK opens the form again with what was typed. The answers last as long as the settings. The backup is unchanged by a login given again while off (`backupDoc` strips it: the next upload answers `same`).

**Built in step 32d4c: the way back for guides** (`apps/tv/src/settings`: `guides-state.ts`'s `GuideRow.needs`, `menuActions`' `link`, `linking`, `linkedElsewhere` and the add form's link mode; `SettingsScreen.tsx`'s `GuideItem` (exported), `menuLabels`, `addFormText`, `AddFormView`; `settings-state.ts`'s `openGuides`; `apps/tv/src/backup`: `backup-start.ts`'s `guides` and `BackupStartEnd.toGuides`, `BackupStartApp.tsx`'s line; `main.tsx`'s `toGuides`; the catalogs; `e2e/backup-logins.spec.ts`'s Arabic guide, `e2e/backup-logins-xa.spec.ts`'s guides walk). **Decided in step 32e: logins on the TV are not encrypted at rest** (ADR-013; Omar's choice on 2026-11-19, relay leg 115):
- **Why not the seal:** the login is in plain text in every stored stream URL (`catalog_chunks`: an Xtream item's `url` is `/live/<user>/<password>/<id>.ts`, an M3U item's is the provider's as given), in `sources_meta.epgUrls` and in the profile (the sources, the guides, `player.last.item`, `channels[].item`). And `localKey`'s master is cached beside them in `kv`. A seal that meant something would cover every copy, under a key not stored on the TV. That is 4 to 6 legs, plus a device-ID read and a decrypt before each start's first play. It would protect only a TV that allowed the device ID, and only against someone without the TV.
- **What holds instead:** everything that leaves the TV is sealed. The pairing is end to end. The backup is sealed under `encKey`, without the logins when "Include logins and links" is off. Our servers never hold a login in plain text (the "no plaintext" tests of steps 31e and 32d1). Logins are never logged or sent as telemetry (step 33's scrubber).
- **`localKey`** stays in `backupKeys`, derived and unused: the vectors and the old engines' checks pin it.
- **The words:** never "encrypted on the TV"; say "encrypted end to end whenever they leave the TV". No code changed; the comments that said "step 32 encrypts it" now point to ADR-013.

**C. Config signing**
- **Keys.** An offline Ed25519 private key is kept in your password manager plus a paper backup. The app embeds two public keys so the key can be rotated.
- **Signing.** `tools/sign-config` signs `config/config.json` and the result is committed to the repo, which gives an audit trail.
- **Doc contents:**
  - `minVersion` per platform
  - `kill[]` rules, each with `{match:{platform, versions, countries}, mode: notice | block-new-sources | disable-playback | disable-app}`
  - `blocklist:{salt, hashes[]}`
  - feature flags and tunables (buffer seconds, watchdog thresholds, EPG windows, telemetry sampling)
  - `issuedAt` and `expiresAt`
- **Fetching.** The app fetches the config at launch without blocking (3 s timeout) and every 6 h. It verifies the signature, caches the result, and falls back to the cached copy if a fetch fails.
- **Blocklist matching.**
  - The client compares `sha256(salt + host)` against the host and each parent domain.
  - It checks when a source is added, on refresh, and before playing. Both the API host and the final stream host after redirects are checked.
  - The list never names domains publicly, and our server never learns which provider a user has.

**Built in step 29b1: `@lablo/crypto` and its shared vectors** (`packages/crypto/src`: `bytes.ts`, `hash.ts`, `backup.ts`, `pairing.ts`, `sign.ts`, `blocklist.ts`, `random.ts`, `check-vectors.ts`; `packages/crypto/test`: `make-vectors.ts`, `vectors.json`; the vectors page `apps/tv/src/vectors`, built into `dist/tv-vectors`; `tools/engines/src/vectors.ts`):
- **The libraries, as pinned:** tweetnacl 1.0.3 (2020) and @noble/hashes 2.4.0 (2026-08-27). Both pure JavaScript.
- **The wire form:** every key, nonce, token and ciphertext is unpadded base64url, decoded strictly (no padding, no `+/`, no length 4k + 1, no non-zero unused bits), so one byte string has one spelling and ids compare as strings. Labels, salts and secrets are UTF-8. The functions take and give bytes.
- **The functions:**
  - `sha256`, `hmacSha256`, `hkdfSha256` (no salt = HKDF's zero salt), `scrypt` (async, yields every 10 ms, reports its progress);
  - backup: `backupSalt`, `deriveBackupMaster` (scrypt N = 2^15, r = 8, p = 1), `backupKeys` (`lookupId` and `writeToken` as base64url, `encKey`, `localKey`), `tokenHash` (what the server keeps of a 32-byte token: b64url(SHA-256(its bytes)); for the write token and pairing's poll token), `sealBackup` / `openBackup` (the envelope `{v: 1, kdf: "scrypt-15-8-1", nonce, ct}`; the gzip of the profile is the caller's, step 32);
  - pairing: `newBoxKeyPair`, `sealPairPayload` (`{phonePk, nonce, ct}`, a fresh phone key pair and nonce each time) / `openPairPayload`; the file in 1 MiB `secretbox` frames: frame i's nonce is the 24-byte base plus i, read as a little-endian number with carry (as libsodium's `sodium_add`); `sealFile` / `openFile` check the whole length against the plain size from the boxed payload, so frames can't be swapped, dropped or cut off; `sealFileFrame` / `openFileFrame` for frame-by-frame use (step 31);
  - config: `verifySignature(message, signature, publicKeys)`: Ed25519 against any of the embedded keys, false (never a throw) for a signature or key of the wrong length; `signKeyPairFromSeed` and `signDetached` for `tools/sign-config` (step 30). tweetnacl's verify doesn't reject a non-canonical S (malleability), which a config signature doesn't care about;
  - `blocklistHash(salt, host)`: b64url(SHA-256(UTF-8(salt + host))); the host's normalisation and its parents are step 30's;
  - `randomBytes`: the platform's `crypto.getRandomValues` through tweetnacl; it throws where there is none.
- **The shared vectors** (`packages/crypto/test/vectors.json`, ASCII, 18 KB): made by `make-vectors.ts` from a second implementation for every answer: Node's own crypto (OpenSSL) for base64url, SHA-256, HMAC, HKDF, scrypt (RFC 7914's two vectors and the backup's N = 2^15), the X25519 and Ed25519 public keys and the Ed25519 signatures; tweetnacl called directly for `box` and `secretbox`; BigInt for the frame nonces. The backup chain from a device secret to the envelope, the pairing payload, a three-frame file whose nonce base carries through eight bytes, the verify answers (rotation, wrong key, changed message or signature, a short signature, no keys), the blocklist. `checkVectors` (its own entry point, `@lablo/crypto/check-vectors`) runs 88 checks over it.
- **Where they run:**
  - Node (`pnpm test`): the file must equal the generator's output, every check passes, and one changed value fails exactly the checks that read it (22 cases);
  - `test/crypto-vectors-bundle.test.ts`: the vectors worker built by the TV pipeline, run in a realm without `BigInt`, `globalThis` or the ES2017 APIs Chromium 53 lacks;
  - the vectors page (`dist/tv-vectors`, built by `pnpm build:tv`): in the page and in a Blob worker, from `file://`; `e2e/crypto-vectors.spec.ts` in current Chromium, `pnpm verify:engines` on Chromium 53, 56 and 68 (each check with Node's answer under Node's name, the worker run in a realm without a document; the self-test's `vectors-seed-changed` must fail).
- **On the old engines (2026-11-17, this PC):** every check in the page and in the worker on Chromium 53 and 68 (88/88 on 53 in the last run, with the self-test's seeded page failing as it must). The backup's scrypt (N = 2^15) took 0.8–0.9 s on Chromium 53 and 0.1 s on 68. A 2018 TV's CPU is several times slower than this PC's: step 32 derives the master in a worker, once, with its progress shown.
- **For the TV bundle (step 30):** @noble/hashes 2.4.0's top-level `BigInt` calls (`_u64.js`, `sha2.js`'s SHA-512 tables) are `/* @__PURE__ */` and tree-shaken out of a bundle that only uses SHA-256; the only `BigInt` left is core-js's to-bigint helper inside a function. noble's scheduler (`nextTick`) reads `globalThis`, so noble needs core-js's `es.global-this` on Chromium 53 (it can't join `POLYFILL_FREE_DEPENDENCIES`). The app doesn't import crypto yet: its bundles are unchanged (219 core-js modules).
- **On CI (the engines job):** 88/88 in page and worker on all three engines; the backup's scrypt 1.0 s on Chromium 53, 3.1 s on 56 (Samsung Tizen 4's engine: its V8 is the slowest of the three here) and 0.1 s on 68. A 2018 Samsung TV could take well over 10 s: step 32 shows progress and derives the master once.
- **Merged:** PR #94 as merge commit `c8267ab` on green CI on the first run (verify 10 m 25 s, web 49 s, media 11 m 56 s, engines 18 m 22 s: all 114 runs as expected, 83 seeded failures caught; the vectors 88/88 in page and worker on Chromium 53, 56 and 68, the backup's scrypt 1.0 s on 53, 3.1 s on 56, 0.1 s on 68 on the CI runner).

**Built in step 30a: the signer, the config files and `GET /v1/config`** (`tools/sign-config`: `sign-config.ts`, `src/config.ts`, `src/cli.ts`; `config/config.json`, `config/config.signed.json`, `config/keys.json`; `packages/core/src/blocklist-host.ts`; `apps/web/src/server/config.ts`, `src/app/v1/config/route.ts`; runbook [config.md](runbooks/config.md)):
- **The files:** `config/config.json` is the doc without its dates (what people edit); `config/config.signed.json` is `{doc, sig}`, the dated doc's exact text (compact JSON) and its Ed25519 signature; `config/keys.json` is `{production, dev}`, the public keys. Git history is the audit trail.
- **`pnpm sign-config`:** `keygen --out` (a new seed, `lablo-config-seed-v1:` and 43 base64url characters, written only outside the repo and never over a file; prints the public key), `pubkey`, `sign --key | --dev [--days N]` (`issuedAt` now, `expiresAt` 180 days on by default, 1 to 400; refuses a doc `configDocSchema` refuses, any field it would strip, dates in the source, an unsorted or repeated blocklist; the key must be trusted), `verify` (a trusted signature, a valid doc, the same doc as `config.json`, not expired; prints the days left), `block` / `unblock` (the normalised host's `blocklistHash` under the doc's salt, the list kept sorted and unique). The salt is fixed for good: the repo has only hashes, so the plain list stays offline with the orders behind it.
- **The keys:** the dev key's seed is SHA-256 of `lablo.config.dev-key.v1`, public on purpose; `keys.json` names its public key. The production keys (current and next) are empty until step 34's key ceremony, done by Omar on his own machine (the seeds never reach the repo, a chat or CI). While there are none, the dev key signs and is trusted; once there are, `sign --dev` and a dev-signed file are refused. The committed file is dev-signed now (`pnpm test` checks it is `config.json` signed by a trusted key, without its date, so it can't fail on a calendar day).
- **The host form** (`@lablo/core`, shared with the TV in step 30b): `blocklistHost` lowercases ASCII, drops one trailing dot, keeps an IPv6 address in brackets, and refuses a port, path, login, empty or over-long label (63) or host (253) and anything non-ASCII (an operator gives the `xn--` form; the TV converts first, step 30b). IP addresses are taken as written. `blocklistCandidates` gives the host and each parent down to the last label; an IP address alone.
- **`GET /v1/config`:** `{doc, sig, country}`. The signed file is imported into the function (checked by `signedConfigSchema` when it loads); `country` is Vercel's `x-vercel-ip-country` when it is two capital letters, else `null`, unsigned (it only picks rules, over HTTPS). `cache-control: private, max-age=300` instead of §3.2's "5-minute CDN cache": the answer carries the caller's country, so a shared cache must not keep it; the doc is inside the function, so an answer costs only the call. `access-control-allow-origin: *` (public, no credentials; the desktop dev app is another origin). `apps/web` now depends on `@lablo/protocol` and `@lablo/crypto` (`transpilePackages`; Turbopack compiled them without it too). CI's `web` filter gained `config/`.
- **Tests:** `blocklist-host.test.ts` 8; `tools/sign-config` 49 (`config.test.ts` 30: the dev key and signatures against Node's OpenSSL, seed files, `keys.json`, dating, every refusal, verify's refusals, the blocklist against Node's SHA-256; `cli.test.ts` 17 on a throwaway repo: each command, a key inside the repo, an existing seed file, production keys turning the dev key away, expiry, `block` then re-sign; `test/committed.test.ts` 2); `apps/web/test/config.test.ts` 9 (the country rules, the exact doc and signature, the headers, a broken file refused at load, the route's answer passing `configResponseSchema` with a trusted signature).
- **Mutation:** `artifacts/mutate30a.py`, 96 written first, 96 caught. Writing the list first took out three conditions nothing could notice (`blocklistHost`'s empty-host check, a "production keys exist" test before the dev key, `insideRepo`'s `relative === ''`) and closed three gaps (an IPv6 address with dots has no parents, a 33-byte key, the days left rounding down).
- **Verified live:** `next start` and requests without the header (`country: null`), with `AE`, and with `ae` (`null`): each 200, `configResponseSchema` valid, the signature good against `keys.json`, `private, max-age=300`.
- **Not yet:** the TV's client and the embedded keys (step 30b), enforcement (step 30c), the production keys and a production-signed config (step 34), a deployment (step 34).
- **Merged:** PR #98 as merge commit `79f834b` on green CI on the first run (verify 9 m 40 s, web 37 s, media 9 m 35 s, engines 20 m 45 s).

**Built in step 30b1: the config client's pure parts and its service** (`packages/data/src`: `config-policy.ts`, `blocklist.ts`, `config-check.ts`, `config-service.ts`; `packages/crypto/package.json`; runbook [config.md](runbooks/config.md) "On the TV"). Nothing in the app uses them yet (step 30b2 wires them in), so the TV bundles are unchanged.
- **`@lablo/crypto` is side-effect free** (`"sideEffects": false`): a bundle keeps only the modules whose exports it uses. tweetnacl itself isn't marked, so wherever `random.ts` is kept, tweetnacl's module body still hands it the platform's `getRandomValues` (`test/crypto-vectors-bundle.test.ts`: a bundle of `randomBytes` alone gives random bytes in a Chromium 53 realm and refuses without a CSPRNG; a bundle of `blocklistHash` alone has none of tweetnacl). Leg 83 measured what this saves: the blocklist check costs the query worker 8.2 KB instead of 40.3 KB.
- **The policy** (`configPolicy(doc, {platform, version, country})`): the strongest matching rule other than a notice, on a ladder where each mode includes the weaker ones (`block-new-sources` < `disable-playback` < `disable-app`; the doc's first among equals; `policyStops(policy, mode)` asks "this mode or stronger"); the matching notices in the doc's order; `updateRequired` below the platform's `minVersion`; the flags and tunables. `compareVersions` reads major.minor.patch with `charCodeAt` (no `split`); a version it can't read compares as `NaN`, so no rule naming versions matches and no update is asked for. A rule naming countries needs a known country. The desktop dev app is the `browser` platform: rules without a platform only, no `minVersion`.
- **The blocklist** (`Blocklist`, `urlHost`, `asciiHost`, `punycode`): `urlHost` reads a URL's host the way browsers do for the cases that matter (tabs and line breaks dropped, blanks trimmed, `\` and any number of slashes after the colon for http, https, ws, wss and ftp, the last `@`, IPv6 brackets) without `URL` (core-js's URL polyfill is about 25 KB). `asciiHost` decodes percent escapes and, for a non-ASCII host, applies UTS #46 simplified: NFKC, each code point lowercased (a whole-word `toLowerCase` turns a final capital sigma into `ς`), the ignored code points dropped (13 ranges: Node's 15 less U+3164 and U+FFA0, which NFKC turns into U+1160), NFC, the ideographic full stop as a dot, each non-ASCII label as `xn--` + RFC 3492 punycode; then `blocklistHost`. The hashes sit in an `Object.create(null)` table (no `Set`, so no polyfill), and an empty list answers without hashing.
  - **Against Node** (Node 24's, the current UTS #46: its `domainToASCII` uses Ada 3.4's IDNA tables, not ICU's; CI runs Node 22, whose older tables map U+1E9E to "ss" and refuse some code points the current one ignores, so the hand-picked answers and the ignored ranges are pinned as literals and compared with Node only when Node maps U+1E9E to the sharp s; the random hosts avoid those code points and run against any Node): RFC 3492's samples; every code point of the BMP and the astral ignored ranges (the set Node's ICU 78 ignores: comparing found eight ranges the first list missed and parts of two more); fifteen hand-picked letters a simple mapping gets wrong; 4,000 seeded random hosts of mixed scripts, capitals, full-width letters, ligatures, ignored code points and odd dots, equal to `blocklistHost(url.domainToASCII(host))` wherever Node answers. The order matters: lowercasing before NFKC gets U+03F9 (the capital lunate sigma) wrong, and the final NFC changes 47 code point and mark pairs.
  - **Known gaps** (documented, not built: a TV can't afford case-folding tables, and no Latin host has them): UTS #46 case-folds beyond lowercase for the Greek letters with an iota subscript, U+0345, the lunate and mathematical final sigmas, the old Cyrillic variants U+1C80 to U+1C88 and Cherokee, and some marks newer than Unicode 15 normalize differently. IP addresses are taken as written (step 30a's rule).
- **The response check** (`checkConfigResponse(text, keys, cachedIssuedAt, now)`): `not-json`, `bad-response`, `bad-signature`, `bad-doc`, `expired` (`now > expiresAt`), `older` (`issuedAt` below the cached copy's; equal is taken); an `issuedAt` in the TV's future is fine (a clock behind refuses nothing). `readCachedConfig(record, keys)` re-checks a cached copy's shape, signature and doc against this build's keys, never its age: an expired cached doc stays in force until a newer one arrives, so a kill can't lapse while the TV is offline.
- **The service** (`ConfigService`, over a `ByteTransport`, a `Clock` and the database): `load(target)` gives the cached copy's policy without the network; `refresh(request)` fetches `<base>/v1/config` within `CONFIG_TIMEOUT_MS` (3 s for the whole answer; Chromium 53 can't abort a fetch, so an answer arriving late is cancelled when it comes) and `CONFIG_MAX_BYTES` (4 MiB), checks it, writes the verified copy to `kv` under `CONFIG_KEY` (`config`: `{doc, sig, country, fetchedAt}`), and answers `{status: fresh | cached | none, policy, issuedAt, expiresAt, failure, stored}`. Any failure keeps the cached copy (or the defaults); a good answer IndexedDB can't keep still applies for the run (`stored: false`): a kill must not wait on storage. `CONFIG_REFRESH_MS` (6 h) is for step 30b2's schedule.
- **Merged:** PR #100 as merge commit `32ae130` on green CI on the third run (verify 10 m 57 s, web 39 s, media 12 m 5 s, engines 20 m 5 s). The first two runs failed `verify` on the IDNA oracle: CI's Node 22 has older IDNA tables than Node 24 (see the tests above).
- **Decided while building (not gates):** the ladder (each mode includes the weaker ones); a country rule needs a known country; equal `issuedAt` taken; the expired cached copy stays in force; 4 MiB cap; the IndexedDB `kv` store of version 1 holds the copy (no schema change); the punycode and mapping on the TV rather than relying on `URL` (size) — with the known gaps above.

**Built in step 30b2: the config client in the app** (`packages/data/src`: `config.worker.ts`, `config-constants.ts`, `config-service.ts` `configHandlers`, `query-service.ts` `blocked`/`reloadConfig`, `blocklist.ts` `cachedBlocklist`, `client.ts` `createConfigClient`; `apps/tv/src/data`: `config-schedule.ts`, `engine.ts` `refreshConfig`/`blocked`, `workers.ts`; `apps/tv/src/main.tsx`; `tv-defines.config.ts`; runbook [config.md](runbooks/config.md) "On the TV"). No UI yet: step 30c enforces the policy.
- **The config worker** (`@lablo/data/config.worker`, 133.7 KB of the 150 KB worker budget): the schemas, the Ed25519 check and the service, which main can't take (leg 83: +89.6 KB). It is short-lived like the ingest worker: `DataEngine.refreshConfig(request, onCached)` starts one, its `run` reads the cached copy and pushes its policy (`cached`), then fetches and answers, and the worker is terminated. The run carries the build's keys (base64url; a run without a key, or with one that isn't 32 bytes, is refused before anything is read), the API base, the platform and the version, so the worker has no build constants of its own. It runs beside the refresh slot (one small record), and never starts the query worker: a running one reads its blocklist again afterwards (`reloadConfig`; a failure there doesn't fail the run).
- **A cached copy this build doesn't trust is deleted** (`ConfigService.readCache`): a key rotated out, a dev-signed copy once a build trusts only production keys, a broken record. The query worker reads the blocklist from the same record without a signature check, so it must never see a copy the config worker would refuse.
- **The query worker's blocklist** (`blocked(urls)` → one boolean per URL): `cachedBlocklist(record)` reads the salt and the hashes from the record's signed text with `JSON.parse` and hand checks (no zod in this worker; anything broken gives an empty list), read when first asked and again at `reloadConfig`; a failed read is tried again on the next call. It costs the query worker 12.5 KB (116.6 → 129.0 KB): the hash (leg 83's 8.2 KB) and the host mapping. The bundled query worker needs `TextEncoder` now (the hash's UTF-8): Chromium 53 has it in workers.
- **The schedule** (`createConfigSchedule`): the first run after the start's first screen has painted (an animation frame, then a task; started where main.tsx shows onboarding or the live screen), then `CONFIG_REFRESH_MS` (6 h) after a run with a new doc, `CONFIG_RETRY_MS` (30 min) after one without (offline at boot, a time limit, a refusal) or whose worker died. Hidden, a due run waits for `visible`; a TV that slept past the due time runs as it wakes; one run at a time. The policy lives in a signal (`config.policy`, the defaults until the cached copy's arrives); `config.last` keeps the last answer.
- **The build's constants** (`tv-defines.config.ts`, used by tools/build's replace plugin and Vite's `define`, declared in `env.d.ts`): `__CONFIG_KEYS__` (a TV build: `config/keys.json`'s production keys, or the dev key while there are none, never both; the desktop dev app: both), `__API_BASE__` (`http://127.0.0.1:3100` until step 34 sets `PRODUCTION_API_BASE`), `__APP_VERSION__` (`apps/tv/package.json`). Tizen's `config.xml` already has `<access origin="*">` (spike S7) and webOS needs nothing for HTTP, so the API host needs no manifest change.
- **Sizes:** main.js 417.7 / 405.0 KB (+2.0 KB: the schedule, and `es.regexp.flags`, which Babel adds by name for the policy's `flags` field; 222 core-js modules, 219 before); the config worker 133.7 KB; the query worker 129.0 KB; the ingest and EPG workers unchanged. The lab's main.js inlines the config worker too (it uses `createAppDataEngine`); its own code is unchanged.
- **Vite pre-bundles the config worker's dependencies** (`optimizeDeps.include`: zod/mini, tweetnacl, the five noble modules): its start-up scan doesn't follow `?worker` imports, and a dependency found late reloads every open page (the fflate lesson): a cold-cache check without them reloaded the app mid-run.
- **Merged:** PR #102 as merge commit `adc2029` on green CI on the first run (verify 11 m 10 s with `pnpm e2e` 128 passed, media 11 m 47 s, engines 19 m 50 s).

**Built in step 30c1: the config plumbing for the tests and the old engines** (`apps/tv/src`: `data/params.ts` `readParams`/`readChecks`/`readApiBase`, `data/config-schedule.ts` `refreshNow`, `test-hook.ts` `configView`, `hello-config.ts`, `DataPanel.tsx`, `main.tsx`, `env.d.ts`; `tools/engines`: `src/config.ts`, `src/fixture.ts`, `src/scenario.ts`, `docker/serve-fixture.sh`, `verify-engines.ts`; `e2e/config.spec.ts`; runbooks [config.md](runbooks/config.md) "On the TV" and old-engines.md item 4). Nothing is enforced yet (steps 30c2 and 30c3).
- **`?api=<origin>`** (`readApiBase`): the config request's base for this run, read before `source` like the other debug parameters (hand-parsed). Only an origin is taken (`http(s)://host[:port]`, one trailing slash dropped); the desktop dev app takes any, a TV build only `127.0.0.1`, `localhost` or `[::1]`, so a store build's address can't send the app to another server. main.tsx builds one `configRequest` for the schedule and the hello screen.
- **`refreshNow()`** on the schedule: a run now, or right after the one in progress (the calls made during a run share the next one, since the served config may have changed since that run began); it resolves with the run's answer (`null` when the worker died, before `start()` and once disposed, and a dispose settles the waiting calls), the schedule counts from its end, and hidden doesn't hold it back. The first-paint run is skipped when a `refreshNow` has already run.
- **The test hook** (`window.__LABLO_TEST__`, every build): `config()` (the last answer as plain data: status, failure, `issuedAt`, the kill rule's id and mode, the notices' ids, `updateRequired`; no texts) and `refreshConfig()` (`refreshNow`). The refresh is the same fetch the schedule makes every 6 h.
- **The hello screen's rows** (`hello-config.ts`, pure): one config run (`config-status` with `data-status`, `data-failure`, `data-kill`, `data-notices`), then `blocked` for each `?check=` URL (`blocked-status`, `data-blocked="true,false"`). The hello screen still starts no schedule.
- **The old-engine harness serves a config:** `serve-fixture.sh` answers `GET /v1/config` from the staged `v1-config.json`, which `src/config.ts` signs with the dev key (one notice rule; two blocked hosts under the harness's own salt, one given in its `xn--` form and checked in Unicode). The hello runs carry `api=` the fixture origin and three `check=` URLs. `evaluateConfig` expects `fresh`, the notice and `true,true,false` while `config/keys.json` has no production keys, and the copy refused (`bad-signature`) with nothing blocked once it has them. **First run on the real engines (this PC, 2026-11-17): the config worker verified, stored and applied the copy, and the query worker answered `true,true,false`, on Chromium 53 and 68, both platforms** (`verify:engines`: all 18 runs as expected).
- **The walk waits for the config run before leaving a page** (found on CI's first run): on Chromium 53, a walk that failed right after a relaunch (the seed that deletes the database at every start) left that page while its config worker (started after the first paint since step 30b2) was in IndexedDB, and every later delete of the database stayed `pending` until the job's time ran out. The schedule now reports `idle()` (no run in progress, none due from `start()`), the test hook `configIdle()`, and `src/onboarding.ts` waits for it (`CONFIG_IDLE`, at most 10 s) before a relaunch and before deleting the database; the stuck-focus seed passes it on.
- **Merged:** PR #104 as merge commit `44baff9` on green CI on the second run (verify 11 m 9 s, its `pnpm e2e` all passed; media 10 m 45 s; engines 19 m 28 s: all 114 runs as expected on Chromium 53, 56 and 68, 83 seeded failures caught, the hello rows `config fresh, blocked true,true,false` on every engine; `web` skipped by its path filter). The first run: `engines` timed out (the Chromium 53 stall, fixed in `f1f47a1`) and `verify` failed the throttled search spec's throttle proof.
- **Sizes:** main.js 420.9 / 408.2 KB (+3.2 KB: the readers, `refreshNow` and `idle`, the hook's view, the hello rows), 222 core-js modules (unchanged); the workers unchanged.

**Built in step 30c2a: the config gate (`disable-app`, the update screen, the notices)** (`apps/tv/src/gate/`: `gate-state.ts`, `GateApp.tsx`, `gate.css`; `main.tsx`; the catalogs' `gate.*`; `packages/data`: `config-constants.ts` `isRuleId`/`MAX_NOTICES`, `profile.ts` `noticesShown` and the `notice` edit; `e2e/config-gate.spec.ts`, `e2e/config-server.ts`; runbook [config.md](runbooks/config.md) "What the TV shows"). `block-new-sources` and `disable-playback` are step 30c2b's.
- **The view** (`gateView(policy, shown, version)`, pure): `disable-app` first (the doc's message, or the catalog's), then the update (`updateRequired`, with this version and the platform's `minVersion`), then the first notice with a message that the profile hasn't shown, in the doc's order. A notice without a message never shows.
- **The gate over every screen** (`createGate`, `GateApp`): its own root after `#app` (the gate's CSS at z-index 20, over the app's dialogs) and its own navigator with one button. While a view shows it takes every key on a capture listener on the window, calling `stopImmediatePropagation`, so the app's remote wiring (a bubbling listener on the window, `connectRemote`) and a text field under it hear none; the wheel too; the Magic Remote's pointer works on its button. Opening it blurs the focused element (an edit ends). OK or Back closes a notice (its id goes into the profile) or, on the other two views, calls `exitApp` (`exitRequested`: the desktop tab can't close).
- **The app under `disable-app` and the update** (`stopped(true)`): the player closes (`Player.close`: the live list on its row) and the catalog refreshes stop. The config schedule goes on: a refresh without the rule closes the gate (`stopped(false)`), the refreshes start again and the app is where it was. At a start whose cached copy carries the rule, the gate shows after the first paint (the config never delays the start), so the last channel may begin to play for a moment before it closes.
- **The language:** `<html lang>` (onboarding and the live screen set it from the language picked), else the TV's (`Platform.info`), in the TV's region; the doc's message in that language (English for any other, the pseudo-locale's included).
- **Where:** onboarding and the live screen; `gate.start` with the profile's `noticesShown` comes right before `config.start()`. The hello and gallery screens have neither.
- **The profile:** `noticesShown` (optional on version 7, no migration), the latest `MAX_NOTICES` (100, as many as a config has rules); the `notice` edit adds an id the protocol's `killRule` would take (`isRuleId`, `charCodeAt` only: a regex's `.test` could pull a polyfill), once.
- **Tests:** unit 5,344 (+59): `gate-state.test.ts` (16: each view and their order, the keys swallowed with their listener options, OK and Back per view, the stop and restart calls, the lift, the pointer, dispose, the default blur), `gate-screen.test.ts` (6: each view's texts in English, Arabic and the pseudo-locale), `config-constants.test.ts` (2: `isRuleId` equal to the schema over every code point to U+017F and 2,000 seeded ids), `profile.test.ts` (+3). e2e `config-gate.spec.ts` (5): a notice over onboarding once (kept, and nothing after a reload), the notice and `disable-app` in Arabic, `disable-app` arriving through `refreshConfig()` while a channel plays (the player closed, no key reaching the live screen, Back asking to leave, the lift giving the keys back), and the update screen in the built webOS bundle from `file://` in English and Arabic.
- **Sizes:** main.js 427.3 / 414.6 KB (+6.4 KB: the gate, its CSS and its texts in both catalogs), 222 core-js modules (unchanged); the query worker 129.4 KB (+0.4: the `notice` edit).
- **Merged:** PR #106 as merge commit `ef641d1` on green CI on the first run (verify 13 m 54 s, its `pnpm e2e` 136 passed; media 10 m 45 s; engines 18 m 27 s: all 114 runs as expected on Chromium 53, 56 and 68, 83 seeded failures caught; `web` skipped by its path filter).

**Built in step 30c2b: `block-new-sources` and `disable-playback`** (`apps/tv/src/gate/gate-state.ts` `refusalFor`; `onboarding/onboarding-state.ts`, `OnboardingApp.tsx`; `player/player-state.ts`, `PlayerScreen.tsx`, `player.css`; `main.tsx`; the catalogs' `error.refused`, `player.refused.*`; `e2e/config-modes.spec.ts`; runbook [config.md](runbooks/config.md) "What the TV shows").
- **The rule** (`refusalFor(policy, mode)`, pure): the kill rule's id and message when it stops `mode` (`block-new-sources` or `disable-playback`) or is stronger, else `null`. main.tsx makes one computed signal per mode from `config.policy` and hands them to onboarding and the player (`refusal`).
- **Onboarding** (`block-new-sources`): "Add source" (once the form's own checks pass) and "Try again" read the signal first. Refused, the loading screen takes `{status: 'refused', refusal}` at once: "Couldn't add {name}", the rule's message in the UI's language (else the catalog's `error.refused`), "Edit details" and "Try again". Nothing loads (`loader.start` isn't called) and nothing goes into the profile. A load running when the rule arrives finishes (the source was asked for before it). Onboarding is the only way to add a source so far (the lab's S6 spike adds one too: a developer tool, left as it is).
- **The player** (`disable-playback`): each play reads the signal before the controller is asked: `playRow` (a channel, a zap, a number, PRE-CH, catch-up, the start's last channel) and `playFilm` (a movie, an episode, "Resume", "Try again", the next episode). Refused, nothing plays (`plays` and the last channel stay; refused before any play, the controller isn't even made), `refused` holds the rule, and the card shows it with one button ("Channels", or "Back" for a movie) that closes the player; the info bar stays up with it, and the track menu won't open. The player follows the signal while it is open: a rule arriving keeps a movie's place, drops a waiting zap, the position reads, the next episode's countdown and the track menu, puts the card up and stops the stream (`controller.stop()`, last: the stopped stream's `idle` then finds the card shown, and `onStatus` keeps it while `refused` holds). A lift changes nothing on screen; the next play that goes through clears `refused`, and its `opening` takes the card away (`onStatus`). With no picture under it, the refused screen paints its own black (`player__blank`: refused before any play, no stage exists and the page would show white).
- **The start:** the last channel plays before the cached policy arrives (`config.start()` after the first paint), so under a cached `disable-playback` it may play for a moment before the rule stops it, as for `disable-app`.
- **Tests:** unit 5,378 (+34): `gate-state.test.ts` (+2: `refusalFor` on the ladder), `onboarding-state.test.ts` (+7: an Add refused before any load and with no answer saved, a rule without a message, the form's own checks first, "Try again" refused then let through after a lift, a failed load's "Try again" refused once the rule arrives, a load running when the rule arrives finishing), `player-state.test.ts` (+13: every way a channel and a movie open refused with the card and no `controller.play`; the rule arriving while a channel, a movie, the track menu, the error card and an episode's countdown show; what was on its way dropped; the bar coming back; the lift; dispose), `catalogs.test.ts` (+12: the three new texts). e2e `config-modes.spec.ts` (3): `block-new-sources` served from the start (the Xtream form's Add refused with the doc's message, nothing in the profile, the rule lifted by `refreshConfig()` and "Try again" adding the source), `disable-playback` arriving through `refreshConfig()` while a channel plays (the controller idle, the `<video>` without its `src`, the card with the doc's message, a zap and a channel from the list refused, the lift letting the next one play), both in Arabic (the refused screen painting its own black).

**Built in step 30c3a: the blocklist at add and on refresh, and where it shows** (`apps/tv/src/data/engine.ts` `refresh`, `refreshEpg`, `BlockedSourceError`, `sourceUrlOf`; `onboarding/loader.ts`, `engine-loader.ts`, `OnboardingApp.tsx`; `data/refresh-scheduler.ts`; `settings/sources-state.ts`, `settings-state.ts`, `guides-state.ts`, `SettingsScreen.tsx`, `settings.css`; the catalogs' `error.blocklisted`, `guides.blocked`, `settings.sources*`, `sources.*`; `e2e/config-blocklist.spec.ts`; runbook [config.md](runbooks/config.md) "What the TV shows").
- **One place checks: the data engine.** `refresh(job)` takes the refresh slot first (a second refresh called meanwhile is refused as before), then asks the query worker's `blocked` for the source's API URL (`sourceUrlOf`: an Xtream account's `base`, an M3U link's `url`); a blocked one rejects with `BlockedSourceError` (`code: 'blocklisted'`) and no ingest worker starts. So every catalog refresh goes through it: onboarding's add, the schedule's, the hello screen's and the lab's S6. `refreshEpg` asks `blocked` for every listed guide's URL: a blocked guide is left out of `only` (with `due` and without) but stays listed, so the EPG worker neither reads nor deletes it. When `blocked` itself fails (the query worker can't read the config record), nothing counts as blocked, with a warning: the blocklist is then unknown, and a broken worker is no reason to refuse a user's source.
- **At add:** `LoadErrorCode` gains `blocklisted` (`blocked` keeps its meaning: an account the provider banned); `loadError` passes it on. The failed-load screen: "Couldn't add {name}", `error.blocklisted` ("This server can't be used with Lablo."), "Edit details" and "Try again" (a refresh that lifts the block lets "Try again" through). The refusal comes in the `checking` phase, before any request to the provider; nothing goes into the profile.
- **On refresh:** the schedule's catalog refresh of a blocked source is refused by the engine and kept as a failure like any other (tried again after `CATALOG_RETRY_MS`: a lift is picked up within 2 h, or at the next start), without the console warning. Its stored catalog stays and plays (30c3b refuses its streams).
- **Where it says so:** Settings gains a second row, "Sources" (after "TV guides", so the guides screens' key paths stay), opening the sources screen (`sources-state.ts`, `SourcesPage`): each stored source in the profile's order, its name (data: `dir="auto"`), its kind and server ("Xtream login · host:port", the host and port only, never a login, path or query: `shownHost`), and one line: "Updated {day}, {time}" (the catalog's `checkedAt`, else `updatedAt`; none with no catalog), "Not updated yet", or for a blocked source, in the warning colour, "Not updated since {day}, {time}: this server can't be used with Lablo." (or "Not updated: …" when it never was). It reads `profile`, `sourceStatus` and `blocked` at each opening (an older opening's answer dropped); no actions yet (OK does nothing, Back returns to Settings). The guides screen asks `blocked` for its rows' full links with each read: a blocked guide's row says "Not read: this server can't be used with Lablo." in place of its status and failure lines.
- **Rows keep two lines:** a settings, guide or source row is 120 px, and a third 38 px line doesn't fit (it was clipped in the first screenshots). The blocked text therefore replaces the line under the name or link. A guide that is read and whose last try failed still shows three lines (from step 22b2: its failure line is clipped), left for a later step.
- **Not covered:** the host an M3U link redirects to (the ingest follows redirects); a guide link typed in onboarding is checked when it is read, not at add. Playing from a blocked server: step 30c3b.
- **Tests:** unit 5,462 (+84): `test/tv-data-engine.test.ts` (+4: a playlist on a blocked host and a panel on a blocked parent refused with no worker started and the slot free, a free source still loading; let through when the blocklist can't be read; a blocked guide neither read nor deleted, with and without `due`, and no worker for it alone; `sourceUrlOf`), `engine-loader.test.ts` (+1 and `loadError`'s `blocklisted`), `refresh-scheduler.test.ts` (+1: a quiet failure held 2 h, the other source refreshed, other failures still warned), `guides-state.test.ts` (+4: the rows' flags in order, the screen's ask with the full links, an unknown blocklist; the settings' two rows and "Sources" by keys, its dispose), `sources-screen.test.ts` (new: the layout numbers, `shownHost`, `sourceRows`, the list's reads, failures, re-openings and dispose, the rows' and the page's drawing in English and Arabic), `unmatched-screen.test.ts` (the sources' reads in `data-pending`). e2e `config-blocklist.spec.ts` (3): the mock's `127.0.0.1` blocked from the start (the Xtream form's Add refused, nothing in the profile, lifted by `refreshConfig()` and "Try again" adding it); a source blocked after it was added (Settings → Sources and Settings → TV guides say so, the lift takes both lines away); both in Arabic.
- **Mutation:** `artifacts/mutate30c3a.py`, 51 written first (writing them closed two test gaps: an older opening of the sources screen asking the blocklist, and the settings' dispose not reaching it): 50 caught (`mutate30c3a.log`); the one that lived, a test gap (the guard on the blocklist's answer when the screen was reopened or disposed while it was out), closed, the rerun `mutate30c3a-b.log` 1/1.
- **Sizes:** main.js 437.7 / 425.0 KB (+7.5 KB: the sources screen, the checks, the en and ar texts), 222 core-js modules (unchanged); the workers unchanged.

**Built in step 30c3b: the blocklist before playing** (`packages/player/src/controller.ts` `blocked`, `checkBlocklist`, `refuseBlocked`; `failure.ts` `blockedFailure`, `BLOCKED_CODE`; `apps/tv/src/player/app-player.ts`, `player-state.ts` `BLOCKLIST_REFUSAL` and the `blocklist` option, `PlayerScreen.tsx`; `data/engine.ts` `blockedOrNone` made public; `main.tsx`; the catalogs' `player.blocked.*`; `e2e/config-blocklist-play.spec.ts`, `e2e/config-server.ts`'s served files; runbook [config.md](runbooks/config.md) "What the TV shows").
- **One place checks: the player controller** (like 30c3a's data engine). Every play of the app (a channel, a zap, a number, PRE-CH, catch-up and its seeks by URL, the start's last channel, a movie, an episode, "Resume", "Try again", auto-next) and every retry rung goes through `PlayerController.open`, which asks `ControllerOptions.blocked([url])` for the URL about to open **while the old connection closes** (close-before-open already waits; a zap waits longer only when the worker answers slower than the close). On Tizen, the redirect probe's answer is asked about too when it differs from the URL. A blocked URL: no `engine.load`, no retry rung (a request's other URLs share the server), no connectivity or account check: the session gives up at once with `blockedFailure()` (`{category: 'blocked', code: 'CFG-BLOCKLIST', detail: 'BLOCKLIST'}`). A rejected check blocks nothing (the app passes `DataEngine.blockedOrNone`, which warns). A zap, a stop or a hide before the answer drops it (the load token).
- **A server blocked while its stream plays:** `checkBlocklist()` asks about the URL on show and the probe's answer for it (the session's `url`), and stops a blocked stream the same way (its tracks emptied, the watchdog gone; while the app is hidden, nothing is left to resume). Not between loads (the next open asks) nor after a VOD's end. player-state calls it at each change of its `blocklist` option, `config.last` in main.tsx, while the player is open: each config answer, after the query worker has read the new list (30b2's `reloadConfig`). Decided: a block is a kill for that server, so it reaches a stream on show within one config refresh, as `disable-playback` does.
- **The card:** player-state turns a `blocked` failure into 30c2b's refusal (`refuse`: the menu, a waiting seek and the next episode's countdown go, a movie's place is kept, the stream stops, `player__blank` paints black) with `BLOCKLIST_REFUSAL` (`id: 'BLOCKLIST'`, which no rule id can be: they are lowercase), so `data-rule="BLOCKLIST"` tells it apart, and its own texts, "Can't play this" / "Its server can't be used with Lablo." (`player.blocked.*`), with one button ("Channels" or "Back"). A `disable-playback` rule arriving over it takes its place. The error card's text table has the same texts for the new category, never shown in the app.
- **Unlike `disable-playback`,** the check is asynchronous and inside the controller, so a blocked play has already counted in `plays` and, for a live channel, become the profile's last channel (`tuned`): the next start shows the card for it.
- **Not covered:** where `<video>` follows redirects itself (webOS, the desktop) only the URL asked for is checked; an HLS stream's variant playlists and segments on other servers; a movie's subtitle files.
- **The e2e's two hosts:** the mock's URLs follow the Host header, so `config-blocklist-play.spec.ts` reads the mock's M3U on `127.0.0.1` (its streams there) and the config server serves it as `localhost:<port>/list.m3u`: the source's link isn't blocked, its streams are.

### 3.4 Telemetry

- **Events:** `app_start` (tier, cold-start ms, catalog size bucket), `ingest_done` (kind, ms, size buckets), `play_result` (engine, container, ttffMs, error class and code), `stall` (recovered, attempts), `epg_match` (rate bucket), `capability` (model, firmware, Chromium version, spike-style feature probes), `crash` (scrubbed stack).
- **Never collected:** URLs, hostnames, channel or VOD names, credentials, or IP addresses.
- **Identity:** a random installId that changes after a wipe.
- **Scrubbing:** the server drops fields not in the schema, and a URL/credential scrubber with tests acts as defense in depth.
- **Control:** sampling per event via config; an opt-out toggle.
- **Reporting:** saved SQL views to start with.

**Built in step 33a: the scrubber and the telemetry API** (`packages/protocol/src/scrub.ts`, the `@lablo/protocol/scrub` entry point and its lint rule; `apps/web/src/server/telemetry.ts`, `src/app/v1/telemetry/route.ts`; runbook `web.md` "Telemetry"):
- **The scrubber** (`scrubText(text, known)`), schema-free and import-free, so the TV's workers take it without zod. Its rules, in order:
  1. Every URL becomes `[url]`, except the app's own script frames: a `file://` URL keeps its file's name and position (`main.js:1:2345`), and a Blob worker's reads `worker:1:2`.
  2. The `known` strings (the TV's own logins, links and hosts) are matched exactly, longest first, from 4 characters. They run after the URLs, so no part of a URL is left around one.
  3. Credential pairs (`password=`, `token=` and the like, and their JSON form) keep the key and read `[x]`.
  4. An Xtream path without its URL loses its two login segments.
  5. E-mails become `[email]`.
  6. IPv4 addresses (with a port), then IPv6 ones (the full form, or any with `::`), become `[ip]`.
  7. Dotted names whose last label is a top-level domain become `[host]`: a list of the common generic ones, plus every two-letter label but `js` (a superset of the country codes), with a letter before it.

  The answer is cut to 4,000 characters (the `crash` event's `stack`). An input over 16,000 is cut first, and the token at that cut dropped. It over-redacts on purpose: `item.tv` reads `[host]`. A channel or programme name has no shape to find; step 33c checks that the app's own errors carry none.
- **The cost on a TV:** bundled alone through the TV pipeline it is 25.8 KB, mostly core-js's string and regex polyfills for Chromium 53. Steps 33b and 33c scrub in a worker, not in main.js (25 KB left on Tizen).
- **`POST /v1/telemetry`:** the rate limit first (60 posts in 10 minutes per caller, through `rateKey`), then the body (64 KB, 413; JSON and `telemetryBatchSchema`, 400). `readTelemetryEvents` reads each event on its own: one it can't read is dropped and counted. Each crash's stack is scrubbed again. The sweep runs before the write: at most 1,000 rows received 90 days ago or more, oldest first. Then one `createMany`: a row per event with its `data` (its fields but `kind` and `at`), `at`, and `receivedAt` from the server's clock. The answer is `{state: 'stored', stored, dropped}`, with the TV's CORS and `no-store`, or 503 with only the error's kind logged. No migration: step 29a's table has every column.
- **Tests:**
  - `scrub.test.ts`, 19 tests: each rule on stacks shaped like Chromium 53's and 68's, and fast-check properties (plain text comes back unchanged; a URL's secret, a known string, an IPv4 address, a host and a pair's value never get through, wherever they sit; idempotent; the cap);
  - `test/scrub-bundle.test.ts`: the bundle in the Chromium 53 stand-in realm, without zod, gives Node's answers;
  - `telemetry.web.test.ts`, 10 tests against Postgres: the rows; the dropped events; the scrubbed stack; 413 at one byte past 64 KB; 400; 429; the sweep's edge and order; no address, URL, host or login in any row; the route's preflight, services, clock and 503;
  - `apps/web/e2e/telemetry.spec.ts` over HTTP from `Origin: null`;
  - **Merged:** PR #143 as merge commit `9f109b6` on green CI (verify 16 m, web 3 m, engines 27 m; media on its rerun after the `epg-declared-300mb` timing flake);
  - mutation `artifacts/mutate33a.py`: 87 written first. 84 were caught on the first run. The three survivors were two test gaps (a key, `signature=`, no test used; a full IPv6 address whose last group the port rule swallowed) and dead code (lower-casing `file://` and `blob:` before a case-sensitive pattern), which was taken out. Then 86/86.

**Built in step 33b1: the TV's sender and the start events** (`packages/data/src/telemetry-service.ts`, `telemetry.worker.ts`, `createTelemetryClient`; `packages/protocol/src/telemetry-buckets.ts`, the `@lablo/protocol/telemetry-buckets` entry point; `apps/tv/src/telemetry/telemetry-state.ts`, `telemetry-events.ts`; `DataEngine.sendTelemetry`; the profile's `telemetry`; `main.tsx`; the test hook's `sendTelemetry`; `e2e/telemetry.spec.ts` and the config server's stand-in; runbook [web.md](runbooks/web.md) "What the TV sends"):
- **Main only records.** `createTelemetry` keeps plain event objects in memory (at most 200, the oldest dropped) and hands them to a telemetry worker at each send. Main takes the event types with `import type` and the buckets from the schema-free `@lablo/protocol/telemetry-buckets`, so no zod reaches main.js. The server checks the events.
- **A worker of its own, started for each send** (66.2 KB of its 150 KB budget; the backup worker is 136.9 KB and holds keys). Its one call, `send`:
  1. **The switch:** the stored profile's `telemetry: false`, read raw, deletes the queue, the install ID and the last capability, and answers `off` with nothing posted. A new ID comes when it is on again, so rows from before and after can't be linked.
  2. **The install ID** (`kv` `telemetry.id`): 16 bytes from `crypto.getRandomValues`, base64url, made at first need. It is never in the backup.
  3. **Sampling** per kind from the config's `telemetrySampling` (a kind with no rate is kept), decided per install: `fnv1a(id + ':' + kind) / 2^32 < rate`.
  4. **`capability`** is queued only when it differs from the last one queued (`telemetry.capability`: the app version, model, firmware, Chromium major and probes).
  5. **The queue** (`telemetry.queue`): the newest 200, written with the ID in one transaction.
  6. **The post:** the oldest events whose JSON fits in the 64 KB body with the envelope (counted in UTF-8 bytes, at least one), 20 s for the answer. 200 takes them off the queue; 400 and 413 too, since the server will never take them; anything else keeps them.
- **When** (`telemetry-state.ts`, as the backup's schedule): from the live screen on (nothing leaves the TV while onboarding is still telling the user about it); 60 s after the first event not yet sent, never sooner than 15 minutes after the last send began; once more 15 minutes after a send that left a queue; once at once when the app is hidden; one at a time; a dead worker's events go back. `disable-app` stops it. Settings' "off" (step 33b3's row calls `setOn(false)`) drops what is held and sends once, after any send in progress, so the worker clears the queue and the ID. An app ended between the switch's edit and that send keeps them on the TV, never sent while off.
- **The start's events,** recorded once the start's screen has painted (the first animation frame after it renders):
  - `app_start`: the tier, `performance.now()` there, rounded and capped at an hour (a backup look-up or "Welcome back" before it included), and the stored items' bucket;
  - `capability`: the model and firmware with characters outside the protocol's set turned into `_` (at most 64, `unknown` when empty), the Chromium major when the schema takes it, and four probes: `year` (when known), `lowTier`, `mse`, `hevc` (`canPlayType` of an HEVC MP4). The screen reader's state stays out: it is a disability signal.
- **The dev app** reports `platform: 'browser'`, which the API refuses (400), so dev rows never reach the table. That is why there is no real-API TV spec: plain e2e reads the batches with the server's schema, the platform swapped, and 33a's `e2e:web` covers the route.
- **The test hook:** `sendTelemetry()` sends now, and telemetry's worker counts in `backupIdle()`, which every e2e wipe and the old-engine harness already wait for.
- **Tests:**
  - unit: `telemetry-service.test.ts` 20, `telemetry-state.test.ts` 15, `telemetry-events.test.ts` 8, the profile's edit, `test/tv-data-engine.test.ts` +2, `test/telemetry-worker-bundle.test.ts` (the bundle in the Chromium 53 stand-in realm: a 500 kept, then posted with the same ID, read whole by `telemetryBatchSchema`; off posts nothing), the hook, the buckets' subpath and its lint rule;
  - e2e `e2e/telemetry.spec.ts` (3): the start's events posted from the live screen, read whole, with no login, link, host or port of the playlist's in any string; the same ID and no repeated `capability` after a restart; a 500 kept and sent again; the switch off clearing the queue and the ID; a start with it off making none; a kind sampled at 0 never sent.
- **Sizes:** main.js 478.6 KB on Tizen (+3.9 KB) and 465.9 KB on webOS. Its new files add no core-js module (`es.array.push` and `es.promise` were there). The worker pulls the typed arrays and `es.json.stringify`.
- **Mutation:** `artifacts/mutate33b1.py`, 179 written first (writing them closed nine test gaps and took out fifteen dead checks); 176 caught on the first run, two gaps closed and one equivalent removed: 178/178.
- **Merged:** PR #144 as merge commit `3e00eec` on green CI on the first run (web 9 m, verify 15 m, media 30 m, engines 32 m).

**Built in step 33b2: the events of use** (`packages/player/src/controller.ts`'s `report` and `PlayerReport`; `apps/tv/src/telemetry/usage-events.ts`; `DataEngineOptions.onRefreshed` and `RefreshDone` in `apps/tv/src/data/engine.ts`; `appPlayer`'s `report`; `main.tsx`; runbook [web.md](runbooks/web.md) "What the TV sends"):
- **`play_result` and `stall` come from the player controller** (`ControllerOptions.report`), the only place that knows a play's start, its first frame, the `PlaybackError` behind a give-up and the engine that played. A play is a session, from `play()` to the next `play` or `stop`, and it reports once:
  - at its first frame: `ttffMs` from `play()` on the controller's clock (a play hidden while it opens starts that clock again on show);
  - or at its give-up before any frame: `ttffMs: null` and the last error.

  A play left before either (a zap, a stop) reports nothing, and neither does one the blocklist refuses: that is the config's verdict, and an event would tie the install to a blocked provider. `engine` is the engine's `kind` at that moment, `container` the URL's (`other` for a request with no URL), `mode` the request's.
- **A stall** is any failure after the play showed a frame: the retries under "Reconnecting…". It reports at its end: the next first frame (`recovered: true`) or the give-up (`recovered: false`), with the retries it took. A resume after a hide is no stall, nor is a buffering the stream gets over; a stall left by a zap or a stop reports nothing. A reporter that throws is logged and changes nothing in playback.
- **The events in main** (`usage-events.ts`, plain functions, `import type` only):
  - `playEvent`: the time rounded and capped at an hour (`eventMs`, shared with `app_start`); the error's kind as the engine gave it (the engine's seven kinds, containers, modes and engines are the protocol's lists, checked both ways by a type test); its code kept only when it is 1 to 64 letters, digits, `_` or `-`, else `OTHER` (no `.`, though the protocol takes one: a dotted code could be a host; nothing is replaced character by character, since what is left of a URL would still spell its host); `status` only on an `http` error, a whole number from 100 to 599.
  - `stallEvent`: the retries capped at 100.
- **`ingest_done` and `epg_match` come from the data engine** (`onRefreshed`), after each catalog refresh that resolves and each EPG refresh that ran its worker. Every refresh goes through those two: the scheduler, onboarding's loader, the phone's add, a login or a link given again.
  - A catalog's `ingest_done`: `xtream`, or `m3u` for a link and for the phone's file; its time; the bucket of the bytes downloaded; the bucket of every kind's items. A result with no download (a 304) records nothing: the event measures a download and its parse against their size. A same-bytes `unchanged` downloaded and parsed it all, so it counts.
  - Each guide downloaded: `epg`, its time, its bytes, and the programmes it read for the channels it feeds (`summary.programmes`).
  - Failed refreshes record nothing: the schema has no failure field, and a source's failure is the provider's. A failure count would be a later field.
  - `epg_match` after an EPG refresh that swapped a guide in, deleted one or moved one: the bucket of `EpgStatus.matched` (the live channels the loaded guides give a guide, combined) over the stored sources' live channels. Nothing with no live channel. **Refined while building:** the shape counted "Channels without a guide" over every source, which walks every live chunk in the query worker, right when the live screen opens after onboarding. `EpgStatus.matched` is what the query worker already holds, so nothing is walked. A link to a channel a newer catalog dropped still counts until its guide is read again; the bucket stops at all.
  - `recordRefresh` never throws: a count that fails is logged, and what came before it stays recorded.
- **Tests:**
  - unit: `controller-report.test.ts` 14 (the fake engine on a manual clock), `usage-events.test.ts` 17 (each event through the server's `readTelemetryEvents`), `app-player.test.ts` +1, `test/tv-data-engine.test.ts` +1 (a playlist, a small Xtream panel and the playlist's guide: the source types, the results, nothing for a 404, a missing login or a guide refresh with nothing due, and the match through the real query worker: 6 of 9);
  - e2e `e2e/telemetry.spec.ts` +1: onboarding a playlist whose guide (on the stand-in) has one of its two channels posts `ingest_done` for `m3u` and `epg` and `epg_match` `50-74`; OK on a channel whose stream the browser refuses posts `play_result` (`html5`, `ts`, `live`, `ttffMs: null`, `unsupported`, `MEDIA_ERR_SRC_NOT_SUPPORTED`); nothing of the source in any post. Its three older tests now count onboarding's `ingest_done`.
- **Sizes:** main.js 481.6 KB on Tizen (+3.0 KB) and 468.9 KB on webOS; no new core-js module (`usage-events.ts` pulls `es.array.push`, `es.promise` and `web.dom-collections.for-each`, all there before). The workers are unchanged.

**Built in step 33b3: the switch in Settings and the consent screen's note** (`apps/tv/src/settings/telemetry-switch.ts`; `settings-state.ts`'s `telemetry` option and fourth row; `SettingsScreen.tsx`'s `TelemetryRow`, `SettingsView` and `telemetryText`; `telemetry-state.ts`'s `telemetryOn` and `setOn`'s promise; `OnboardingApp.tsx`'s consent note; the catalogs; `main.tsx`; runbook [web.md](runbooks/web.md) "What the TV sends"):
- **The row:** Settings' fourth, "Usage and crash reports", last so that the earlier rows' paths stay. It is a `menuitemcheckbox` in the settings menu, its value ("On" or "Off") beside its name and a line under it:
  - on: "Anonymous: never your links, logins or channel names";
  - off: "Nothing is sent while they are off";
  - a change that didn't take: "Couldn't change it. Try again.", in the warning colour, until the next OK or opening.

  OK switches it in place: no page and no question, since neither way loses anything of the user's ("off" is the private direction, and "on" again is one OK). In Arabic the value is feminine (مفعّلة, متوقفة), agreeing with التقارير, so it has words of its own rather than the backup's.
- **Its value** is read from the stored profile at each opening of Settings (`telemetry !== false`; no profile reads as on, as main's start does through the same `telemetryOn`). The telemetry worker reads that same field before each send, so the row shows what the worker will find. Until the read answers there is no value, and OK reads again; a read that fails only logs.
- **An OK:** the profile's edit first, so that a send between the two finds "off" stored; then the profile read back; then main's telemetry told what it says (`setOn`). The read-back is the truth: `editProfile` answers `false` both when nothing changed and when a newer app's profile refused the edit, and a privacy switch must never show "Off" while the stored one says on. When the stored value isn't what was asked, or the edit fails, the row says so.
- **`setOn` resolves** after "off"'s clearing send (at once while the sends are stopped, and for "on"). The row stays busy until then and OK does nothing: a second OK would store "on" before the worker reads the switch, and the old queue would go out under the old ID, the link between before and after that deleting the ID prevents. The row's requests, the clearing send included, count in the settings screen's `data-pending`.
- **The consent screen's note** sits under "Allow" and "Not now", in the note style with space above it: "Lablo also sends anonymous usage and crash reports to improve the app, never your links, logins or channel names. You can turn them off in Settings." Not between the backup's note and "If you don't allow it…": that sentence right after this one would read as declining the reports, and the buttons answer the TV ID only. Nothing is sent before the live screen (33b1), so the note is shown before anything leaves the TV. Counsel's review of opt-out (above) covers its place too.
- **Tests:**
  - unit: `telemetry-switch.test.ts` 9 (the read, the order of an OK, busy, a refused or failed change, a stale read, disposal), `telemetry-row.test.ts` 5 (the words in en and ar, the row as drawn, its place in the list), `telemetry-state.test.ts` +2 (`telemetryOn`; `setOn`'s promise), `guides-state.test.ts` +1 (the row in Settings), `unmatched-screen.test.ts` (the row's requests in `data-pending`);
  - e2e: `e2e/telemetry.spec.ts` +2, in en and ar: after a refused send, Settings' fourth row switched off in place clears the queue, the ID and the capability's key and sends nothing, reads "Off" at the next opening, and switched on again sends the next start's events under a new ID, with the capability again; `e2e/onboarding.spec.ts` checks the consent note's text under the buttons in en and ar (its en-XA walk and the old-engine walk measure the screen as before); `e2e/backup-logins-xa.spec.ts` walks the row on and off in en-XA (bracketed, uncut).
- **Sizes:** main.js 485.5 KB on Tizen (+3.9 KB) and 472.8 KB on webOS; no new core-js module. 14.5 KB is left on Tizen for 33c's main-side handler and 33d's "Send a report".
- **Mutation:** `artifacts/mutate33b3.py`, 80 written first; 80/80 caught on the first run.
